-formally smooth condition also passes through the limit.
∎

The main reason to define these overconvergent deformation properties is that they hold for solid smooth and étale maps.

Proposition 3.7.5
.

A solid étale morphism of bounded affinoid rings is
\dagger
-formally étale. A solid smooth morphism of affinoid rings is
\dagger
-formal smooth locally in the analytic topology. Moreover, a standard solid smooth morphism of bounded affinoid rings is
\dagger
-formally smooth.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is standard solid smooth or standard solid étale, namely, rational localizations are also written as composite of standard solid étale maps. First, let us show that
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
is
\dagger
-formally smooth, it suffices to prove that
R_{{\square}}\to R\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
is
\dagger
-formally smooth, but this follows from Proposition
2.6.16
(1) and the fact that
R[T]
is a projective animated
R
-algebra. Indeed, if
I\subset\mathcal{A}
is a
\dagger
-nilpotent ideal, then
(\mathcal{A}/I)^{\dagger-\red}=\mathcal{A}^{\dagger-\red}
and a map
\mathbb{Z}[T]\to\mathcal{A}
extends to
\mathbb{Z}[T]_{{\square}}
if and only if it does for
\mathcal{A}/I
.

We are left to show that a standard solid étale morphism of bounded algebras is
\dagger
-formally étale. By writing
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A})
as a sifted colimit of quotients of algebras of the form
R\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[S]\rangle
, we can assume that
\mathcal{A}=R\langle X_{1},\ldots,X_{s}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[S]\rangle
for
S
a profinite set and a finite set of variables
X_{i}
, and that
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
with
a=\mathrm{det}(\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial T_{j}})
a unit. We can also assume that all the
f_{i}
are of norm
\leq 1
and that
|\pi^{k}|\leq|a|\leq 1
for some
k\geq 0
. By Lemma
3.6.11
the map
\SpecAn\mathcal{B}\to\SpecAn\mathcal{A}
is
0
-truncated, then we only need to prove the existence and uniqueness of lifts at the level of points. Let
\mathcal{D}
be a bounded affinoid ring and
I\subset\mathcal{D}
a
\dagger
-nilpotent ideal, consider a solid commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}/I.}

We want to see that there is a unique dashed arrow
\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{D}
making the diagram commutative. By Proposition
2.6.16
we can find a lift
F:\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}\to\mathcal{D}
such that
f_{i}(\underline{T})\in I\subset\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{D})
. Therefore, the map
F
extends to a map

\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\rangle_{{\square}}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{n}\}^{\dagger}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{i}-S_{i})\to\mathcal{D}.

By Lemma
3.7.6
down below, we have an equivalence of
\mathcal{A}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\}
-algebras

\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\rangle_{{\square}}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{n}\}^{\dagger}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{i}-S_{i})\cong\mathcal{B}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\}

which shows that there is a lift
\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{D}
over
\mathcal{D}/I
. Suppose we have two lifts
f_{1},f_{2}:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{D}/I
. Then they extend to a map
\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{D}
, write

f:\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d},S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}/(f_{i}(T),f_{i}(S)),

then the differences
T_{i}-S_{i}
are sent to
I
, and the map
f
factors through the overconvergent diagonal

\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}\{T_{i}-S_{i}\}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{D},

but the proof of Lemma
3.6.11
implies that
\mathcal{B}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{B}\{T_{i}-S_{i}\}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{B}
proving the uniqueness.
∎

The following lemma was used in the previous proposition.

Lemma 3.7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a bounded affinoid ring,
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{A}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}
a solid Tate algebra over
\mathcal{A}
in
d
-variables, and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{D}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
a standard solid étale algebra over
\mathcal{A}
. Let
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}\{f_{i}:i=1,\ldots,d\}^{\dagger}
denote the idempotent algebra associated to the closed subspace
\Spa\mathcal{B}\subset\Spa\mathcal{D}
, i,e, the base change

\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{D}\otimes_{R_{{\square}}[S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}]}R\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\}^{\dagger}

mapping
S_{d}\mapsto f_{d}
. Then there is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{A}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\}
-algebras

\mathcal{B}\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{d}\}\cong\mathcal{C}.

Proof.

By writting
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A})
as a sifted colimit of quotients of algebras of the form
R\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle
for finite set of variables
\underline{X}
and profinite sets
K
, we can assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{A}=R\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle
, that the
f_{i}
have norm
\leq 1
and that
g=\mathrm{det}(\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial T_{j}})_{1\leq i,j\leq d}
satisfies
|\pi^{k}|\leq|g|\leq 1
for some
k\geq 0
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{0}=R^{+}\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle
,
\mathcal{D}^{0}=\mathcal{A}^{0}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}
and
\mathcal{B}^{0}=\mathcal{D}^{0}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
. For
n\geq 0
let
\mathcal{C}_{n}^{0}=\mathcal{D}^{0}\langle\frac{S_{1}}{\pi^{n}},\ldots,\frac{S_{d}}{\pi^{n}}\rangle/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{i}(T)-S_{i})
and set
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\mathcal{D}_{n}^{0}[\frac{1}{\pi}]
. The explicit Koszul resolution of the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{B}^{0}/\mathcal{A}^{0}}
shows that multiplication by
\pi^{k}
is homotopic to
0
. On the other hand, for
n\geq 2k+1
the map

\mathcal{D}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}

factors trough
\mathcal{B}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
, namely,
S_{i}=\pi^{n}\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{n}}
vanishes in the quotient. By lemma
3.7.7
down below we have a lift
\mathcal{B}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}
, and by the uniqueness of lifts shown at the end of Proposition
3.7.5
(which is independent of the existence of a lift), we have a natural lift in generic fibers
\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{C}
independent of
n
. Now, let us fix some
n\geq 2k+1
and a lift
\mathcal{B}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}
, and for all
m\geq n
take
\mathcal{B}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{m}
to be the composite of
\mathcal{B}^{0}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{m}
. We can extend these maps to morphisms
\mathcal{B}^{0}\langle\frac{S_{1}}{\pi^{m}},\ldots,\frac{S_{d}}{\pi^{m}}\rangle\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{n}
of
\mathcal{A}^{0}\langle\frac{S_{1}}{\pi^{m}},\ldots\frac{S_{d}}{\pi^{m}}\rangle
-algebras. For
m^{\prime}>m\geq n
these algebras factor through

\mathcal{B}^{0}\langle\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}\rangle\to\mathcal{B}^{0}[[\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}]]\to\mathcal{B}^{0}\langle\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m^{\prime}}}\rangle

and

\mathcal{C}^{0}_{m}\to\mathcal{D}^{0}[[\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}]]/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{i}(T)-S_{i})\to\mathcal{C}^{0}_{m^{\prime}}.

Then, we have a map of
\mathcal{A}^{0}[[\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}]]
-algebras

(3.9)

\mathcal{B}^{0}[[\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}]]\to\mathcal{D}^{0}[[\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}}]]/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{i}(T)-S_{i}).

Both terms in (
3.9
) are
I=(\frac{S_{i}}{\pi^{m}})
-complete, and their reduction modulo
I
is an equivalence, this implies that (
3.9
) is an equivalence. Taking generic fibers and colimits as
m\to\infty
one gets the lemma.
∎

The following lemma is substracted from the proof of
[
Sch15
, Corollary III.2.2]
.

Lemma 3.7.7

(Quantitative Hensel’s Lemma)
.

Let
A\to B
be a morphism of
\pi
-complete animated
R^{+}_{{\square}}
-algebras such that there is some
k\geq 1
such that multiplication by
\pi^{k}
on
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is homotopic to
0
. Let
C
be a
\pi
-complete animated
A
-algebra, and suppose that we have a solid commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{2k+1}}

Then there is a dashed arrow as above making the diagram commute.

Proof.

It suffices to construct a sequence of compatible arrows
B\to\mathcal{C}/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
for all
n\geq 0
. Suppose that we have the lift for
n\geq 2k+1
, we will construct a lift for
2(n-k)\geq n+1
over
n-k
. Consider the algebra

C^{\prime}=C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{2(n-k)}\times_{C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}}C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n},

then the fiber
C^{\prime}\to C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
is equivalent to
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}
under the map
\iota=(\pi^{n-k},0):C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}\to C^{\prime}
. Moreover, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{2n-k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where
\Delta
is the diagonal map. Let us write
J=C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}
for the square zero ideals of the algebras
C^{\prime}
and
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{2n-k}
over
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
. Then, the map
\Delta
induces a morphism

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A},J)\xrightarrow{\pi^{k}}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A},J),

which is homotopic to zero as the multiplication by
\pi^{k}
is homotopic to zero on
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
by hypothesis. By deformation theory, we deduce that the obstruction to lift
B
from
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
to
C^{\prime}
vanishes, but lifting from
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n}
to
C^{\prime}
is equivalent to lifting from
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{n-k}
to
C/^{\mathbb{L}}\pi^{2(n-k)}
, which proves the lemma.
∎

4.
Cartier duality for vector bundles

After all the preliminaries in derived algebraic geometry, we are finally in shape of applying the theory to more interesting objects. In this section we study Cartier duality for different incarnations of vector bundles, following the spirit of
[
Lau96
]
, but using the language of six-functor formalisms and the Lu-Zheng category. In the next sections we shall apply these results to study different incarnations of the de Rham stack.

4.1.
Vector bundles and torsors

First, let us briefly introduce the category of vector bundles on solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks.

Definition 4.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}={\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff_{\mathbb{Z}_{{\square}}})
be the category of solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks.

(1)

Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack, a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
is a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathscr{F}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X)
that is free of rank
d
locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology of
X
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
be the co-cartesian fibration associated to the functor
\Mod_{{\square}}:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\to\CAlg(\mathcal{P}r^{L,\ex})
. We let
\BUN_{d,\mathcal{C}}\subset\mathcal{D}
be the subcategory whose objects are pairs
(X,\mathscr{F})
with
\mathscr{F}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X)
a vector bundle of rank
d
, and morphisms
(Y,\mathscr{G})\to(X,\mathscr{F})
given by the space of connected components
(f:X\to Y,f^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{F})
such that
f^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{F}
is an equivalence.

Let
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
be the linear algebraic group of
d\times d
invertible matrices over
\mathbb{Z}
, and let
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
be its classifying stack. By definition,
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
is the object representing the moduli problem of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-torsors on
\mathscr{D}
-stacks. Over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
we have a vector bundle
\St
associated to the standard left representation of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]}
be the slice category of
\mathscr{D}
-stacks over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, the vector bundle
\St
induces a functor

F:(\mathcal{C}_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]})^{\op}\to\BUN_{d,\mathcal{C}}.

by taking pullbacks.

Proposition 4.1.2
.

The functor
F
is an equivalence of categories over
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
.

Proof.

Both
(\mathcal{C}_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]})^{\op}
and
\BUN_{d,\mathcal{C}}
are left fibrations over
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
, by
[
Lur09
, Proposition 3.3.1.5]
it suffices to prove that the fibers over
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
are equivalent. Let
X\in\mathcal{C}
, we want to show that the natural functor

(4.1)

F_{X}:\Map_{X}(X,[X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}])^{\op}\to\BUN_{d,X}

from maps
f:X\to X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
to rank
d
-vector bundles over
X
is an equivalence of anima. To see that
F_{X}
is essentially surjective, note that for
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle over
X
, the stack
\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}_{X}(\mathscr{O}_{X}^{d},\mathscr{F})
of isomorphisms in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology is a
\mathbf{GL}_{d}|_{X}
-torsor over
X
, which is defined by some map
f:X\to[X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]
such that
f^{*}\St=\mathscr{F}
. To show that
F
is fully faithful, notice that both terms in (
4.1
) are Kan complexes, so it suffices to show that for a map
f:X\to X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, the anima of automorphisms of
f
is equivalent to the anima of automorphisms of
\mathscr{F}=f^{*}\St
. It suffices to show that the natural map of stacks

(4.2)

\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}}(X)\to\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})

is an equivalence. Since
f:X\to[X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]
is an epimorphism of stacks, it satsifies universal
*
-descent and it suffices to show that (
4.2
) is an equivalence after pullback along
f
. Let
Y=X\times_{[X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}]}X
, then
g:Y\to X
is a
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-torsor and we the multiplication map gives rise an equivalence
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\times Y\xrightarrow{\sim}Y\times_{X}Y
, one deduces that

\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{X/\mathbf{GL}_{d}}(X)|_{Y}=\mathbf{GL}_{d}\times Y.

Similarly, the pullback of
\mathscr{F}
to
Y
is naturally isomorphic to
\mathscr{O}_{Y}^{d}
and
\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})|_{Y}=\underline{\mathrm{Aut}}_{Y}(\mathscr{O}_{Y}^{d})=\mathbf{GL}_{d}\times Y
. It is clear that the restriction of (
4.2
) to
Y
is identified with the identity of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}\times Y
.
∎

Remark 4.1.3
.

Definition
4.1.1
and Proposition
4.1.2
are not special for the
\mathscr{D}
-topology of the category of solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks. The same can be done for a general Grothendieck topology in a full subcategory of analytic rings stable under pullbacks.

Definition 4.1.4
.

For
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle over
X
, we let
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
denote its geometrization. Explicitly, let
\underline{\St}
be the analytic spectrum of
\mathrm{Sym}\St^{\vee}
, it is naturally endowed with the standard action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and defines a vector bundle
\mathbb{V}(\St)
over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. Let
f:X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
be the map defining
\mathscr{F}
via Proposition
4.1.2
, then
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})=f^{*}\mathbb{V}(\St)
. Note that
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
is the relative analytic spectrum of
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
, which is an analytic ring locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology of
X
.

4.2.
Algebraic Cartier duality for vector bundles

Now that we have related the category of vector bundles of rank
d
and the slice category of
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, we can state our first Cartier duality that is nothing but the algebraic Cartier duality of
[
Bha22
, Proposition 2.2.13]
. In order to simplify the theory, and since our main application will be for rigid spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we will focus in characteristic
0
, though some statements will be proven in general.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
and
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
its geometrization seen as an abelian group object over
X
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{n}
be the relative analytic spectrum of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n}\mathrm{Sym}^{k}_{X}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
. The formal completion of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
at zero is defined as the abelian group stack
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}=\varinjlim_{n}\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{n}
, we let
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})=\varprojlim_{n}\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
denote the global sections of
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
.

The following lemma will be useful to show cohomologically smoothness of classifying stacks.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{C},E)
be a geometric set up and
\mathscr{D}
a six functor formalism on
(\mathcal{C},E)
taking values in stable
\infty
-categories. Let
f:Y\to X
be a map in
E
with
f^{*}
conservative, and let
g:X\to Y
be a retraction of
f
in
E
.

(1)

Suppose we are given with the following data:

(i)

An object
\mathcal{L}\in\mathscr{D}(X)
.

(ii)

A map
s:f_{!}1_{Y}\to\mathcal{L}
.

(iii)

A retraction
1_{Y}\cong g_{!}f_{!}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{g_{!}s}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\eta}1_{Y}
.

Then
1_{X}
is
g
-smooth and there is a natural identification
g^{!}1_{Y}\cong\mathcal{L}
.

(2)

Suppose we are given with the following data:

(i)

An object
\mathcal{L}\in\mathscr{D}(X)
.

(ii)

A map
s:\mathcal{L}\to f_{!}1_{Y}
.

(iii)

A section
1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\mu}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{g_{!}s}g_{!}f_{!}1_{Y}\cong 1_{Y}
.

Then
1_{X}
is
g
-proper and there is a natural identification
\mathcal{P}_{g}(1_{Y})\cong\mathcal{L}
.

Proof.

We only prove part (1), part (2) follows by taking the dual six functor formalism
\mathscr{D}^{\op}
, see
[
Sch23
, Remark 6.5]
.

Let
\Delta:X\to X\times_{Y}X
be the diagonal map. We need to define a cycle morphism
\mu:\Delta_{!}1_{X}\to\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}
such that the following compositions are the identity

(4.3)

1_{X}\cong\pi_{1,!}\Delta_{!}1_{X}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1,!}\mu}\pi_{1,!}\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\cong g^{*}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{g^{*}\eta}1_{X}.

(4.4)

\mathcal{L}\cong\pi_{2,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Delta_{!}1_{X})\xrightarrow{\pi_{2,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mu)}\pi_{2,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L})\cong\pi_{2,!}\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}\cong g^{*}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{g^{*}\eta\otimes\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{L}.

By Lemma
[
Sch23
, Lemma 5.11]
, after modifying
\eta
, it suffices that the composite are equivalences. We have the following commutative diagram with cartesian squares

(4.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{Y}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(fg{,}\id)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

In particular, we have that
\pi_{2}^{*}f_{!}\cong\Delta_{!}g^{*}
, we define
\mu:\Delta_{!}1_{X}\cong\pi_{2}^{*}f_{!}1_{Y}\to\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}
to be
\mu=\pi_{2}^{*}s
.

The composite (
4.3
) is the identity
.
Since
g^{*}g_{!}\cong\pi_{1,!}\pi_{2}^{*}
, the composite (
4.3
) is obtained by applying
g^{*}
to the retraction

1_{Y}\cong g_{!}f_{!}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{g_{!}s}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\eta}1_{Y}.

The composite (
4.4
) is the identity
. Since the pullback along
f:Y\to X
is conservative, it suffices to show that (
4.4
) is an equivalence after taking
f^{*}
. By proper base change we have that
f^{*}\pi_{2,!}\cong g_{!}\Delta^{*}
. On the other hand, the equivalence
\mathcal{L}\cong\pi_{2,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Delta_{!}1_{X})
arises by the
\pi_{2,!}
of the equivalence

\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Delta_{!}1_{X}\cong\Delta_{!}\mathcal{L}\cong\Delta_{!}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L},

taking pullbacks along
\Delta
we get the natural equivalence

\mathcal{L}\otimes\Delta^{*}\Delta_{!}1_{X}\cong\Delta^{*}\Delta_{!}1_{X}\otimes\mathcal{L}

given by the braiding isomorphism. Consider the (not necessarily commutative) diagram

(4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Delta_{!}1_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{!}\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{!}1_{X}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}^{*}s\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}}

Applying
\Delta^{*}
to (
4.6
), and using that
\Delta_{!}g^{*}\cong\pi_{2}^{*}f_{!}
, we get the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\otimes f_{!}1_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}\Delta_{!}\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}\otimes s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\otimes\mathcal{L}}

where the composite of the horizontal maps is the braiding isomorphism. In a similar way, using that
\pi_{2,!}\pi_{1}^{*}\cong g^{*}g_{!}
and
\pi_{1,!}\pi_{2}^{*}\cong g^{*}g_{!}
, one deduces that the equivalence

f^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes g_{!}\mathcal{L}\cong f^{*}(\mathcal{L}\otimes g^{*}g_{!}\mathcal{L})\cong f^{*}\pi_{1,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L})\cong f^{*}\pi_{2,!}(\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L})\cong f^{*}(g^{*}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}

is also the braiding isomorphism. On the other hand, the
f
-pullback of the map
g^{*}\eta\otimes\mathcal{L}
is nothing but the map

g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\eta\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}}f^{*}\mathcal{L}.

Putting all together, the
f
-pullback of the map (
4.4
) becomes

(4.7)

f^{*}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\sim}g_{!}(f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{L})\xrightarrow{g_{!}(s\otimes\mathcal{L})}g_{!}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}(\mathcal{L}\otimes g^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\eta\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}}f^{*}\mathcal{L},

but the map
g_{!}(f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{L})\xrightarrow{g_{!}(s\otimes\mathcal{L})}g_{!}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}
is equal to the composite

g_{!}(f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{g_{!}s\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}}g_{!}\mathcal{L}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L},

where the equivalence
g_{!}(f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong g_{!}f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{L}
arises from the natural isomorphism
f^{*}\pi_{2,!}\cong g_{!}\Delta^{*}
applied to
\pi_{2}^{*}f_{!}1_{Y}\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{L}
. This shows that the composite (
4.7
) is an equivalence, proving what we wanted.
∎

Before we state the algebraic Cartier duality theorem we need to show some cohomological properties of vector bundles. We start with a key lemma that is the core of the computations.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There is a natural de Rham resolution of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
-comodule given by a complete and decrasing filtration

\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}.

(2)

There is a natural Koszul resolution of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
-module given by a complete and increasing filtration

\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{O}_{X},

whose dual is the de Rham complex for
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
.

Proof.

By base change, it suffices to deal with the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
the standard vector bundle. We have
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant de Rham and Koszul resolutions for both
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
and
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)
:

(4.8)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{Q}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\otimes\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to\cdots\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\otimes\St^{\vee}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})\to\mathbb{Q}\to 0

and

(4.9)

\displaystyle 0\to\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to\cdots\to\cdots\to\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\otimes\St\to\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\to\mathbb{Q}\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{Q}\to\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\xrightarrow{d}\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\otimes\St\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to 0

where we have identified the differentials
de\cong e
for
e\in\St^{\vee}
(resp. for
\St
). The resolutions of (
4.8
) are the duals of those in (
4.9
), this prove the lemma since the de Rham resolution of a vector bundle is a complex of comodules while the de Koszul resolution is a complex of modules.
∎

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{{\square}}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

The map
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to X
is weakly cohomologically proper.

(2)

The map
f:\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\to X
is cohomologically smooth and there is a natural isomorphism
f^{!}1_{X}=\Omega^{d}_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}/X}[d]=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
. If in addition
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
then
f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\otimes(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F})[-d]
. In particular,
f_{!}f^{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F}
.

Proof.

Part (1) is clear since
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
is just the relative analytic spectrum of the algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F}
(locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology) with the induced analytic structure.

For part (2), without loss of generality we can reduce to the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
the standard representation. Then, to see that
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
is cohomologically smooth, we can take the pullback along
*\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. In this case,
\St=\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module of rank
n
, and by induction it suffices to treat the case
d=1
. We have a map

j:\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\subset\mathbb{G}_{a}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[T]\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

Note that
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is cohomologically smooth over
\mathbb{Z}_{{\square}}
, namely, it has an open cover in the sense of locale by the solid affine spaces
\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[T]_{{\square}}
and
\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[T^{-1}]_{{\square}}
. Then, it suffices to show that
j
is an open immersion, this follows since it is the complement of the idempotent algebra over
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
given by
\mathbb{Z}[T^{-1}]
.

Next, we show that
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
and
f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F})[-d]
. For this, we can reduce to the universal case of
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
.

Let
\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{d}\}
be the standard basis of
\St
and
T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}
its dual basis. Then

\St=\AnSpec_{X}(\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]).

Consider the idempotent
(\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}],\mathbb{Z})_{{\square}}
-algebras

D_{i}=\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}][T_{i}^{-1}],

and let
C
be the union of the algebras in the sense of locale. More precisely, for
I\subset\{1,\ldots,d\}=:[d]
let
D_{I}=\otimes_{i\in I,\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]}D_{i}
and let
C
be the idempotent
dg
-
\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]
-algebra

C=[D\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}D_{i}\to\cdots\to\bigoplus_{|I|=k}D_{I}\to\cdots\to D_{[d]}].

Then
j:\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}\subset\mathbb{V}(\St)
is the open subspace complement to
C
, and we can compute

f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}=[\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]\to C].

Unravelling the construction of
C
, and identifying
T_{i}^{-1}=v_{i}
, one finds that

(4.10)

f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}=(T_{1}T_{2}\cdots T_{d})^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[T_{i}^{-1}:i=1,\ldots,d][-d].

Note that shifting the variables
T_{i}
and
T_{j}
alters the formulas by a
-1
factor, namely, in the union of idempotent algebras
D_{i}
and
D_{j}
one fixes the map

C\xrightarrow{(1,-1)}D_{i}\bigoplus D_{j},

which differs from the map

C\xrightarrow{(1,-1)}D_{j}\bigoplus D_{i}

under the natural isomorphism
D_{i}\bigoplus D_{j}=D_{j}\bigoplus D_{i}
by multiplication by a
-1
. If
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}
, one deduces that
f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}=\bigwedge^{d}\St\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St[-d]
. In general, using that

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Z}}(f_{!}\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}],\mathbb{Z})=f_{*}f^{!}\mathbb{Z},

we find that
f^{!}\mathbb{Z}
is the localization at
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}(\St)
of the object

f^{!}\mathbb{Z}=T_{1}\cdots T_{d}\otimes\mathbb{Z}[[\underline{T}]][d]

as
\mathbf{GL}_{n}
-equivariant
\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]
-module, which is nothing but
f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d]
.
∎

Next, we study cohomological properties of quotient stacks associated to vector bundles in characteristic
0
. Let us first describe the categories of sheaves on
X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
and
X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
via the monadicity theorem.

Proposition 4.2.5
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There are natural equivalences

\Mod(X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))=\mathrm{CoMod}_{\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}}(\Mod(X))

and

\Mod(X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})})=\Mod_{\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}\mathscr{F}}(\Mod_{X}).

(2)

Consider the maps
X\xrightarrow{f}X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{g}X
. Then
f
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover and
g
is both weakly cohomologically proper and cohomologically smooth. Moreover, there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}1_{X}\cong g^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]
.

(3)

Consider the maps
X\xrightarrow{f}X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\xrightarrow{g}X
. Then
f
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover,
g
is cohomologically smooth, and there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}1_{X}\cong\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[-d]
. Moreover,
g
is co-smooth with proper dual
\mathcal{P}_{g}(1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}})\cong 1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}}[-2d]
.

Proof.

By base change, we can reduce all the assertions to the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
. We first use Proposition
3.1.27
to deduce part (1), and then we apply Lemma
4.2.3
to construct the data required in Lemma
4.2.2
and show (2) and (3).

(1)

By Proposition
4.2.4
the maps
f:X\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
and
f^{\prime}:X\to X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}
are weakly cohomologically proper and cohomologically smooth respectively. Then, by Proposition
3.1.27
and Remark
3.1.28
we have that

\Mod(X/\mathbb{V}(\St))=\mathrm{CoMod}_{f^{*}f_{*}1_{X}}(\Mod(X))

and

\Mod(X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)})=\Mod_{f^{!}f_{!}1_{X}}(\Mod(X)).

It is left to compute the monad and comonad, for this one uses the fact that the Čech nerves of
X\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
and
X\to\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}
are given by the simplicial stack
(\mathbb{V}(\St)^{n/X})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
(resp.
(\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}^{n/X})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
) encoding the commutative group structure of
\mathbb{V}(\St)
and
\mathbb{V}(\St)
respectively, which arise from the
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant Hopf-algebra structure of
\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}\St^{\vee}
and
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}^{\bullet}\St^{\vee}
respectively (see
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.5.2 (3)]
and Proposition
4.2.4
(2)), we left the details to the reader.

(2)

By part (1), the category
\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\mathbb{V}(\St))
is the category of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant (left) comodules over
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
. The map
X\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
is the vector bundle associated to the algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
over
X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
, endowed with the natural comodule action given by co-multiplication.

The map
f
is weakly cohomologically proper since
\mathbb{V}(\St)
has the induced analytic structure from
X
. To show that
g
has
!
-functors, it suffices to prove that
f
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover, which amounts to show that
f_{*}1_{X}
is descendable over
1_{X/\mathbb{V}(\St)}
. For this, we can use the de Rham complex of Lemma
4.2.3

(4.11)

0\to\mathbb{Q}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to 0

which is a
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant complex of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
-comodules, where
\St^{\vee}
has the trivial comodule action (we higlight for future reference that this is equal to the adjoint comodule action since
\mathbb{V}(\St)
is abelian). Note that if
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
is a basis of
\St^{\vee}
, we have identified
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{Q}de_{i}\cong\St^{\vee}
. One formally deduces that
g
is weakly cohomologically proper as
f
is so, see Corollary
3.1.26
. Finally, it is left to show that
g
is cohomologically smooth with
g^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\St[d]
. Let
\mathbb{Q}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\mathbb{V}(\St))
be the trivial representation seen as a morphism in
\LZ_{\mathscr{D},X}(X/\mathbb{V}(\St),X)
. Let
\mathcal{L}:=(\bigwedge^{d}\St)[d]\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\mathbb{V}(\St))
endowed with the trivial comodule action. Twisting (
4.11
) by
\bigwedge^{d}\St
we get a map
f_{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\to\mathcal{L}
. Moreover, (
4.11
) shows that

g_{!}\mathcal{L}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d}\bigwedge^{i}\St[i],

this gives the retraction
g_{!}\mathcal{L}\to 1_{X}
. We conclude by applying Lemma
4.2.2
.

(3)

The map
f
is cohomologically smooth by Proposition
4.2.4
. Since the pullback
f^{*}
is conservative,
f
is in fact a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover, in particular of universal
!
-descent, and thus
g
admits
!
-functors. On the other hand, since being cohomologically smooth is local on the source (Corollary
3.1.26
), we see that
g
is also cohomologically smooth. It is left to compute
g^{!}1_{X}
. By functoriality of the Lu-Zheng category, it suffices to treat the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{n}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
. By Proposition
4.2.5
, one has that
1_{X}=f^{!}g^{!}1_{X}=(f^{!}1)\otimes f^{*}(g^{!}1_{X})=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d]\otimes f^{*}(g^{!}1_{X})
. This gives
f^{*}g^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St[-d]
, and we only need to identify
\bigwedge^{d}\St
as object in
\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)})
. Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}(\St)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

by smooth base change, we have that
g^{!}1_{X}=\Delta^{*}\pi_{2}^{!}(1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}})
. On the other hand, let
Q=\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}/\Delta(\St)
, endowed with the left regular action of
\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}
. The map
\Delta
is equivalent to the map

(4.12)

\widehat{\mathbb{V}(Q)}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})}\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}).

This shows that
\Delta^{!}1_{X/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})}=\Delta^{*}\bigwedge^{d}Q^{\vee}[d]
. One gets that

\displaystyle g^{!}1_{X}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{*}\pi_{2}^{!}(1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}(\St)})

\displaystyle=\Delta^{!}\pi_{2}^{!}(1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}}(\St)})\otimes\Delta^{*}\bigwedge^{d}Q[-d]

\displaystyle=\Delta^{*}\bigwedge^{d}Q[-d]

\displaystyle=\bigwedge^{d}\St[-d].

It is left to show that
g
is co-smooth with proper dualizing sheaf
\mathcal{L}=1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}[-2d]
; we use Lemma
4.2.2
. By part (1),
\Mod(X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)})
is equivalent to the category of
\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}\St
-modules in
\Mod(X)
. The Koszul sequence

(4.13)

0\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St\to\mathbb{Q}\to 0

gives rise a map

1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}[-2d]\to f_{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St[-d].

To construct a retraction
g_{!}\mathcal{L}\to 1_{X}
it suffices to show that the Koszul complex induces a splitting
g_{!}\mathcal{L}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[-i]
. We know by (
4.13
) that
g_{!}\mathcal{L}
is a perfect complex of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces. Then, to show that it is split it suffices to do it for its dual. We have that

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X}(g_{!}\mathcal{L},\mathbb{Q})=g_{*}\mathcal{L}[2d]=g_{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St[d].

But we have that
g_{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St[d]=(g_{*}1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St[d]
, thus, it suffices to show that the cohomology
g_{*}1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}
splits, this follows by taking the de Rham resolution for
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
, since
g_{*}1_{X}
is the “complete” comodule associated to
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
.

∎

Remark 4.2.6
.

In the previous proposition we always keep track of the adjoint action of
\St
, even if it is trivial. The reason is that similar computations will hold for the case of classifying stacks of more general groups, see Proposition
6.1.7
.

Theorem 4.2.7

(Algebraic Cartier duality)
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle over
X
.

(1)

There is a natural bi-linear map

F:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\times_{X}X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}\to*/\mathbb{G}_{m}

functorial in the category
\BUN_{d,\mathcal{C}}
, such that
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is an isomorphism in the Lu-Zheng category, considered as a map
\LZ_{X}(\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}),X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})})
. Moreover, the inverse of
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is naturally isomorphic to
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d]
.

(2)

There is a natural bi-linear map

G:\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}\times_{X}X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})\to*/\mathbb{G}_{m}

functorial in the category
\BUN_{d,\mathcal{C}}
, such that
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is an isomorphism in the Lu-Zheng category considered a a map in
\LZ_{X}(\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})},X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))
. Moreover, the inverse of
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is naturally isomorphic to
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]
.

In particular, we have Cartier duality isomorphisms induced by a Fourier-Moukai transform

(4.14)

FM_{1}:\Mod_{{\square}}(\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})})

(4.15)

FM_{2}:\Mod_{{\square}}(\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})),

defined by the convolution

FM_{1}(M)=F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star M\mbox{ and }FM_{2}(M)=G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star M.

Proof.

By functoriality of the Lu-Zheng category, we can assume without loss of generality that
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and that
\mathscr{F}=\St
is the standard vector bundle. On the other hand, by
[
Sch23
, Lemma 5.11]
it suffices to construct a unit and co-unit for the adjunction and prove that they are equivalences.

Step 1. Construction of
F
and
G
.
We first construct the maps
F
and
G
of parts (1) and (2). Let us start with part (1). Consider the
\mathbf{GL}_{n}
-equivariant vector bundle
\mathbb{V}(\St)=\AnSpec_{X}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee})
, as well as its formal dual
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}
. By Proposition
4.2.5
(1), a line bundle over
\mathbb{V}(\St)\times_{X}X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}
is the same as a
\mathbf{GL}_{n}
-equivariant line bundle over
\mathbb{V}(\St)
endowed with a module action of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
commuting with the
\mathscr{O}(\mathbb{V}(\St))
-linear structure. We simply take
\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
as a right module over
\mathbb{V}(\St)
endowed with the natural left multiplication of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
, we say that
\mathcal{L}
has the
left regular action
. This defines the map
F
.

For the map
G
in (2), we argue in a similar way. A line bundle in
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}\times_{X}X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
is the same as a compatible system of
\mathbf{GL}_{n}
-equivariant line bundles
(\mathcal{L}_{n})
on
\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})_{n}
for all
n
, together with a compatible system of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant comodule structures

(\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{n}).

To define such an object, we take
\mathcal{L}_{n}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\St
endowed with right module structure, and construct the comodule structure of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
by taking adjoints of the multiplication map
\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\St\otimes\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\mathcal{L}_{n}
, namely,

\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{n}.

This defines a line bundle on
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}\times_{X}X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
, and so the map
G
. We say that
\mathcal{L}
has the
left regular action
.

Step 2. Identification of
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))
and
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))
. By construction
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is the line bundle
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
over
\mathbb{V}(\St)
endowed with the left multiplication by
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
. Then,
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))=F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))^{-1}
is the line bundle
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
over
\mathbb{V}(\St)
endowed with the
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
-multiplication arising from the composite

\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{s\otimes\id}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{m}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}

where
s:\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
is the antipode map sending
v\mapsto-v
for
v\in\St^{\vee}
. We say that
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))
has the
right regular action
.

Similarly,
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is the line bundle
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\St)_{n}
over
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}
endowed with the left regular
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
-comodule structure constructed as the adjoint of the multiplication map. Then,
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))
is the line bundle
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\St)_{n}
endowed with the right regular comodule structure of
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}\St^{\vee}
obtained by composing the multiplication map with the antipode. We say that
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))
has the
right regular action
.

Step 3. Unit and co-unit for
F
.
Next, we construct the unit and co-unit maps of the convolutions and see that they are equivalences. We first deal with (1). Let us write
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
,
Y=\mathbb{V}(\St)
and
Z=X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}
. We also let
\mathcal{L}=F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
and
\mathcal{G}:=F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[-d]
. Recall that we consider
\mathcal{L}\in\LZ_{X}(Y,Z)
and
\mathcal{G}\in\LZ_{X}(Z,Y)
, so that we have the convolution

\mathcal{G}\star\mathcal{L}=\pi_{1,3,!}(\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{G})

for the fiber product
Y\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y
, and the convolution

\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}=\pi_{1,3,!}(\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{L})

for the fiber product
Z\times_{X}Y\times_{X}Z
. Thus, we want to construct equivalences

(4.16)

\Delta_{Y,!}1_{Y}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{G}\star\mathcal{L}

and

(4.17)

\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Delta_{Z,!}1_{Z}.

Let us first compute
\mathcal{G}\star\mathcal{L}
. The tensor
\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{L}
lies in
Y\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y=\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}
where the quotient is with respect to the trivial action. By step 2 one deduces that
\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{G}
is nothing but the line bundle
\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee}_{1}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})[-d]
endowed with the
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
-module action which is left regular on
\St^{\vee}_{1}
and right regular on
\St^{\vee}_{2}
. Thus, by taking the anti-diagonal embedding
\Delta^{ant}=(\id,-\id):\St^{\vee}\to\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St^{\vee}_{2}
, we can write

\displaystyle\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee}_{1}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})[-d]

\displaystyle=\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}((\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St^{\vee}_{2})/\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))[-d]

\displaystyle\cong\bigwedge^{d}(\Delta^{ant}\St)\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}((\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St^{\vee}_{2})/\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))[-d]

where we use the composite
\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\Delta^{ant}}\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}\to\St_{2}
to identify
\bigwedge^{d}\Delta^{ant}(\St)\cong\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}
, and the module action of
\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}(\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))
on
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}((\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St^{\vee}_{2})/\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))
factors through the counit. Consider the composite

Y\times_{X}Y\xrightarrow{\tilde{f}}Y\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y\xrightarrow{\tilde{g}}Y\times_{X}Y

arising from the maps
X\xrightarrow{f}Z\xrightarrow{g}X
. By Proposition
4.2.5
we can write

\widetilde{f}_{!}1_{Y\times_{X}Y}=\bigwedge^{d}(\Delta^{ant}\St)\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{ant}\St)\otimes_{1_{\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St)}}}1_{Y\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y}[-d].

Since
Y\to X
is just a vector bundle, it is clear that
\Delta_{Y,!}1_{Y}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}((\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})/(\Delta^{ant}(\St)))
. Thus, we find that

\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{G}\cong\widetilde{f}_{!}1_{Y\times_{X}Y}\otimes\widetilde{g}^{*}(\Delta_{Y,!}1_{Y}).

Applying
\pi_{1,3,!}=\widetilde{g}_{!}
, we get that
\mathcal{G}\star\mathcal{L}\cong\Delta_{Y,!}1_{Y}
, which gives the unit map (
4.16
) that in addition an equivalence.

Now, let us construct the co-unit map for
\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}
. The object
\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{L}
lies over
Z\times_{X}Y\times_{X}Z=\mathbb{V}(\St)/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})}
. By step (2) it is the line bundle
\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}_{1}\otimes(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee})[-d]
, where
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
is endowed with the
\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})
-module structure which is right regular for
\St_{1}^{\vee}
and left regular for
\St_{2}^{\vee}
. Equivalently, let
Q^{\vee}=(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})/(\Delta(\St^{\vee}))
, then

\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{G}=\bigwedge^{d}\St_{1}^{\vee}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}Q^{\vee}[-d]

endowed with its natural
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})
-module structure given by left multiplication. Hence,

\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}=\bigwedge^{d}\St_{1}^{\vee}\otimes(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}Q^{\vee})[-d].

Now, the diagonal map is equivalent to (
4.12
), and Proposition
4.2.4
(2) provides the isomorphism
\Delta_{!}1_{X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}
as wanted.

Step 4. Unit and co-unit for
G
.
Now we move to (2). We set
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
,
Y=\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}
and
Z=X/\mathbb{V}(\St)
. We also write
\mathcal{L}=G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
and
\mathcal{G}=G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St[d]
. We want to construct equivalences (
4.16
) and (
4.17
). In the first case, the tensor product
\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{G}
lies over
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})}/\mathbb{V}(\St)
with quotient given by the trivial action. By step (2), it is described as the line bundle
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}[d])_{n}
endowed with the comodule structure over
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
defined by the composite of the anti-diagonal embedding
\Delta^{ant}=(\id,-\id):\St\to\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}
and the left regular action of
\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2}
. Then, we can write

(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}[d])_{n}=(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}(\Delta^{ant}(\St))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St_{2}[d]\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}((\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})/\Delta^{ant}(\St)))_{n},

so that the comodule associated to the limit of
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}\Delta^{ant}(\St))_{n}
is the dual to the module structure of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{ant}(\St^{\vee}))
.
Let
g:X/\mathbb{V}(\St)\to X
, Proposition
4.2.5
(1) implies

\displaystyle g_{*}((\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee})^{\vee}\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St[d])

\displaystyle=g_{*}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X/\mathbb{V}(\St)}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee},g^{!}\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X}(g_{!}(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}),\mathbb{Q})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Q}.

We deduce that

\mathcal{G}\star\mathcal{L}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}((\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})/\Delta^{ant}(\St)),

which produces the unit map (
4.16
) that is clearly an equivalence. Next, we construct the co-unit (
4.17
), consider the tensor product
\pi_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{G}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{L}
over
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\St^{\vee})}/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})
. By step (2) it consists on the line bundle
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\leq n}_{X}(Q)\otimes\bigwedge^{d}Q[d])_{n}
where
Q=(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})/\Delta(\St)
is endowed with its natural comodule structure of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St_{1}^{\vee}\oplus\St_{2}^{\vee})
given by left regular action. Thus, by Proposition
4.2.4
(2) we get that

\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(Q^{\vee}).

On the other hand, the diagonal map
X/\mathbb{V}(\St)\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{V}(Q)/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})\to X/\mathbb{V}(\St_{1}\oplus\St_{2})
, this shows that
\Delta_{!}1_{X/\mathbb{V}(\St)}\cong\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}Q^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{L}\star\mathcal{G}
as wanted.
∎

Remark 4.2.8
.

The statement and proof of Theorem
4.2.7
also apply for fpqc-stacks in classical derived algebraic geometry. Indeed, the functors and objects involved in the universal case arise from stacks on schemes endowed with the theory of classical quasi-coherent modules. On the other hand, a careful bookkeeping of the construction of the units and co-units should prove that the composites (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) are actually the identity and that
[
Sch23
, Lemma 5.11]
would not be necessary, we left this computation to the curious reader.

We finish this section with some classical properties of the Fourier-Moukai transform of Theorem
4.2.7
.

Proposition 4.2.9
.

Let
X
be a solid
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle of rank
d
. Consider the Fourier-Moukai transforms
FM_{1}
and
FM_{2}
of Theorem
4.2.7
. The following hold

(1)

Let us write
Y=\mathbb{V(\mathscr{F})}
and
Z=X/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}
. Denote
\iota:X\to Y
the zero section, and
p:Y\to X
,
f:X\to Z
,
g:Z\to X
the natural maps. We have the following natural identities of convolutions in the Lu-Zheng category over
X
:

(i)

F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}=1_{Z}

(ii)

\iota_{!}1_{X}\star(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d]=g^{!}1_{X}
.

(iii)

(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\star f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}=1_{Y}
.

(iv)

f_{!}1_{X}\star F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))=1_{Y}
.

(2)

Let us write
Y=\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}
and
Z=X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. Denote
\iota:X\to Y
the zero section, and
p:Y\to X
,
f:X\to Z
,
g:Z\to X
the natural maps. We have the following natural identities of convolutions in the Lu-Zheng category over
X
:

(i)

G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}=1_{Z}

(ii)

\iota_{!}1_{X}\star(G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d])=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]=g^{!}1_{X}
.

(iii)

(G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d])\star f_{!}1_{X}=1_{Y}
.

(iv)

f_{!}1_{X}\star G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))=p^{!}1_{X}
.

Proof.

We only show part (1), part (2) is done in a similar way. The object
\iota_{!}1_{X}
is just
\iota_{*}1_{X}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(Y)
, let us considered it as a morphism in
\LZ_{X}(X,Y)
. Since
\iota
is both cohomologically smooth (by Corollary
3.6.10
), and weakly cohomologically proper (being affinoid with induced analytic structure), it has right and left adjoints given by
\iota_{*}\iota^{!}1_{Y}=\iota_{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[-d]
and
\iota_{*}1_{Y}
respectively.

(i)

Let us compute
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}
, the term
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is seen as a map
Y\to Z
. Consider the fiber product
X\times_{X}Y\times_{X}Z
, by definition
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}=\pi_{1,3,!}(\pi_{1,2}^{*}\iota_{!}1_{X}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1)))
. We obtain that

\pi_{1,2}^{*}\iota_{!}1_{X}\otimes\pi_{2,3}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))=\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes_{\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F})}\mathscr{O}_{X}=\mathscr{O}_{X}

endowed with the trivial comodule structure. Since
\pi_{1,3}:Y\times_{X}Z\to Z
is the base change of
Y\to X
, one deduces that
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}=1_{Z}
as wanted.

(ii)

Next, let us take left adjoints to the expression
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))\star\iota_{!}1_{X}=1_{Z}
, recall that we see
\iota_{!}1_{X}
and
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
as maps
X\to Y
and
X\to Z
respectively, so that
1_{Z}
is seen as a map
X\to Z
. Since
g:Z\to X
is cohomologically smooth,
1_{Z}
is a right adjoint as a map
X\to Z
with left adjoint given by
g^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d]
. One deduces that

\iota_{!}1_{X}\star(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d].

(iii)

Let us consider
f_{!}1_{X}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(Z)
as a morphism
X\to Z
in the Lu-Zheng category over
X
. Since
f
is cohomologically smooth, it is a left adjoint and has by right adjoint
f_{!}f^{!}1_{X}=f_{!}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]
. Consider the fiber product
X\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y=Z\times_{X}Y
, with projections
\pi_{Y}
and
\pi_{Z}
, then

(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\star f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}=\pi_{Y,!}((F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{Z}^{*}(f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z})).

We have a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times_{X}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

By proper and smooth base change we have a natural equivalence
\pi_{Z}^{*}f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}=f^{\prime}_{!}f^{{}^{\prime}!}1_{Y\times_{X}Z}
, which by Proposition
4.2.4
(2) yields that

(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{Z}^{*}f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}=(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\mathrm{Sym}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee}).

where
\mathrm{Sym}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
is endowed with the
left
regular comodule structure. Equivalently, consider the map

Y\times_{X}Z\times_{X}Y\xrightarrow{\pi_{2,3}}Z\times_{X}Y,

then

\displaystyle(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{Z}^{*}f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}

\displaystyle=(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{2,3,!}\pi_{1,2}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))

\displaystyle=\pi_{2,3,!}(\pi_{2,3}^{*}(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))).

One gets that

\displaystyle(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\star f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,!}\pi_{2,3,!}(\pi_{2,3}^{*}(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1)))

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,!}\pi_{1,3,!}(\pi_{2,3}^{*}(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\otimes\pi_{1,2}^{*}F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1)))

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,!}(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\star F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,!}\Delta_{Y,!}1_{Y}

\displaystyle=1_{Y},

proving what we wanted.

(iv)

For the last identity, we take right adjoints to the identity
(F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d])\star f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}=1_{Y}
. Indeed,
1_{Y}
is seen as a map
X\to Y
in the Lu-Zheng category over
X
, and since
p:Y\to X
is weakly cohomologically proper,
1_{Y}
is a left adjoint with right adjoint given by itself. Similarly, since
f:X\to Z
is cohomologically smooth,
f_{!}f^{!}1_{Z}
is a left adjoint when seen as a map
X\to Z
, with right adjoint given by
f_{!}1_{Z}
. One obtains the identity

f_{!}1_{Z}\star F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))=1_{Y}.

∎

Corollary 4.2.10
.

Keep the notation of Proposition
4.2.9
.

(1)

In the conventions of part (1) there are natural equivalences of functors

(i)

FM_{1}\circ\iota_{*}=g^{*}
.

(ii)

\iota^{*}\circ FM_{1}^{-1}=g_{!}(-\otimes g^{!}1_{X})
.

(iii)

FM_{1}^{-1}\circ f_{!}(-\otimes f^{!}1_{Z})=p^{*}
.

(iv)

f^{*}\circ FM_{1}=p_{!}
.

(2)

In the conventions of part (2) there are natural equivalences of functors

(i)

FM_{2}\circ\iota^{*}=f_{!}
.

(ii)

\iota_{*}\circ FM_{2}^{-1}=g_{!}(-\otimes g^{!}1_{X})
.

(iii)

FM_{2}^{-1}\circ f_{!}=p^{*}
.

(iv)

f^{*}\circ FM_{2}=p_{!}(-\otimes p^{!}1_{X})
.

Proof.

This follows by translating the kernels in the Lu-Zheng category over
X
to their associated functors by convolution.
∎

Example 4.2.11
.

We now explain the relation of Cartier duality and the Beilinson
t
-structure. Consider the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
by multiplication, the map
\mathbb{G}_{a}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the standard line bundle over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
and its Cartier dual over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the quotient stack
B(\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a})
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
by multiplication. Similarly, the Cartier dual of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is
B(\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\mathbb{G}_{a})
. The category
\mathrm{Fil}(\Mod(\mathbb{Q}))
has two different
t
-structures, the standard and the Beilinson
t
-structure, it turns out that they are actually the natural
t
-structures of the modules over the stacks
\mathbb{G}_{a}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
B(\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}})
under the Cartier duality isomorphism respectively.

4.2.1.
Solid vector bundles

We finish the section with some short discussion about a variant of vector bundles for solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks, namely, solid vector bundles:

Definition 4.2.12
.

We let
\mathbf{GL}_{d,{\square}}
be the analytic spectrum of the ring
\mathbb{Z}[X_{i,j},Y:1\leq i,j\leq d]_{{\square}}/(Y\mathrm{det}(X_{i,j})-1)
. The category of
solid
vector bundles of rank
d
on solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks is the slice category
{\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff_{\mathbb{Z}_{{\square}}})_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d,{\square}}]}
. A solid vector bundle is denote by
\mathscr{F}_{{\square}}
, where
\mathscr{F}
is the underlying vector bundle associated to the composite
X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d,{\square}}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. Let
\St
be the standard representation of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, then
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{Z}}\St^{\vee})_{{\square}}
has a natural action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d,{\square}}
, which defines an analytic space
\mathbb{V}(\St)_{{\square}}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. For a solid vector bundle
\mathscr{F}_{{\square}}
over a stack
X
, we let
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F}^{\vee})_{{\square}}
and
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{{\square}}
be the pullback of
(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{Z}}\St^{\vee})_{{\square}}
and
\mathbb{V}(\St)_{{\square}}
along
X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
respectively.

We have a partial analogue of Proposition
4.2.4

Proposition 4.2.13
.

Let
X
be a solid stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{{\square}}
and
\mathscr{F}_{{\square}}
a solid vector bundle of rank
d
. Let
f:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{{\square}}\to X
. Then
f
is cohomologically smooth and there are natural equivalences
f^{!}1_{X}\cong f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
.

Proof.

This follows the same proof of Proposition
4.2.4
after taking some modifications on the idempotent algebras
D_{i}
. Indeed, using the same notation as
loc. cit.
consider the idempotent
\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]
-algebras
D_{i}=\mathbb{Z}[[T_{i}^{-1}]][\underline{T}]
, and let
C
be its union in the sense of locale. Then,
\mathbb{V}(\St)_{{\square}}
is the open complement of
C
in
\mathbb{V}(\St)
, and we can compute

f_{!}1_{\mathbb{V}(\St)_{{\square}}}=[\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}]\to C].

An explicit calculation gives that

f_{!}1_{\mathbb{V}(\St)_{{\square}}}=(T_{1}\cdots T_{d})^{-1}\mathbb{Z}[[T_{1}^{-1},\cdots,T_{d}^{-1}]][-d].

Taking duals one finds that

f_{*}f^{1}1_{X}=(T_{1}\cdots T_{d})\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\underline{T}][d],

and that
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d]
.
∎

4.3.
Analytic Cartier duality for vector bundles

We have proven an algebraic Cartier duality for vector bundles, in this section we shall study three additional incarnations of this phenomena in rigid geometry for Tate stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, for some fixed prime
p
. Nevertheless, some of the constructions and statements still make sense for Tate stacks over
(R,R^{+})=(\mathbb{Z}((\pi)),\mathbb{Z}[[\pi]])
, we will make explicit this distinction when necessary.

4.3.1.
Cartier duality for unitary overconvergent vector bundles

In Proposition
4.1.2
we saw that the category of vector bundles of rank
d
on solid
\mathscr{D}
-stacks is equivalent to the category of
\mathscr{D}
-stacks over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. Therefore, in order to construct different incarnations of vector bundles it suffices to construct different incarnations of the group
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
.

Definition 4.3.1
.

Let
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}R\langle\pi^{\varepsilon}T\rangle
be the overconvergent algebra defining the closed disc of radius
1
.

(1)

We define the overconvergent linear group
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
to be the analytic spectrum of the algebra

R\langle X_{i,j},T:1\leq i,j\leq d\rangle^{\dagger}/(\mathrm{det}(X_{ij})T-1)

representing invertible matrices
A
such that
|A|\leq|\pi^{-\varepsilon}|
and
|A^{-1}|\leq|\pi^{-\varepsilon}|
for all
\varepsilon>0
.

(2)

We define the category of unitary overconvergent vector bundles of rank
d
on analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stacks over
R_{{\square}}
to be the slice category
{\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff^{b}_{R_{{\square}}})_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}]}
.

(3)

Given an analytic
\mathscr{D}
stack
X
over
R_{{\square}}
, and a vector bundle
\mathscr{F}
of rank
d
defined by a map
f:X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, a
lattice

\mathscr{F}^{+}
of
\mathscr{F}
is a factorization

X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}.

We also say that
\mathscr{F}^{+}
is an
unitary overconvergent vector bundle
over
X
.

Construction 4.3.2
.

Let
X
be an Tate stack over
R_{{\square}}
. Let
f:X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
be a unitary overconvergent vector bundle of rank
d
, and let
\mathscr{F}
denote the vector bundle associated to the composite
X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
. Similarly as for algebraic vector bundles, we can construct two different geometric incarnations that are analogue to
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
and
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
. Let
\St
be the standard representation of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
with canonical basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
. Let
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\St^{+})}\subset\mathbb{V}(\St)
be the closed subspace given by the analytic spectrum of
R\langle e_{1}^{\vee},\ldots,e_{d}^{\vee}\rangle^{\dagger}
where
e_{i}^{\vee}\in\St^{\vee}
is the dual basis. By construction,
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\St^{+})}\subset\mathbb{V}(\St)
admits a descent datum for the action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
, and thus it defines an analytic space over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
. We define
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}:=f^{*}\overline{\mathbb{V}(\St^{+})}
and call it the
closed overconvergent ball of radius
1
in
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. Dually, let
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+}):=\bigcup_{\varepsilon>0}\pi^{\varepsilon}\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
be the unit open ball in
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. We have a series of inclusions

\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\subset\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\subset\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}\subset\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}).

Remark 4.3.3
.

The map
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
is an open analytic inclusion, namely, locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology it is nothing but the inclusion of the open unit polydisc in the algebraic affine space. In particular, we can always localize modules over
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
to solid sheaves over
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
.

Definition 4.3.4
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{+}
be an overconvergent vector bundle over
X
. We let
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
denote the algebra of functions of
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
seen as an object in
\Mod_{{\square}}(X)
. We also let
\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
denote the global sections over
X
of
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
.

We now prove the analogue of Proposition
4.2.4

Proposition 4.3.5
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
R
and let
\mathscr{F}^{+}
be a unitary overconvergent vector bundle over
X
.

(1)

The map
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}\to X
is weakly cohomologically proper.

(2)

The map
f:\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\to X
is cohomologically smooth and there is a natural isomorphism
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
. If in addition
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
then
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})[-d]
. In particular,
f_{!}f^{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
.

Proof.

Part (1) is clear since
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
is the relative analytic spectrum of the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
endowed with the induced analytic structure.

For part (2), smoothness of
f
follows from Proposition
3.6.13
. The computation of
f^{!}1_{X}
and
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
follows the same lines of the proof of Propositions
4.2.4
and
4.2.13
: we first reduce to the univesal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+}=\St^{+}
, we let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
be the standard basis of
\St^{+}
with dual basis
T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}
. We then define the idempotent
R[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]
-algebras
D_{i}=R\langle T_{i}^{-1}\rangle^{\dagger}[T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}]
. The same computations will show that

f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{+})}=(T_{1}\cdots T_{d})^{-1}R\langle T_{1}^{-1},\ldots,T_{d}^{-1}\rangle^{\dagger}[-d]=\bigwedge^{d}\St\otimes(\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{\vee}))^{\vee}[-d].

If
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
this is precisely
\bigwedge^{d}\St\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+})[-d]
. In general, taking duals one gets that

f_{*}f^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{\vee})[d],

localizing at
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
one gets that
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d]
as wanted.
∎

Before giving a proof of the analogue of Proposition
4.2.5
, we need to find suitable de Rham and Koszul resolutions as in Lemma
4.2.3
.

Lemma 4.3.6
.

Let
X
be an Tate stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}^{+}
be a unitary overconvergent vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There is a natural de Rham resolution of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
-comodule given by the complete decreasing filtration

\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}.

(2)

There is a natural Koszul resolution of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
-module given by the complete increasing filtration

\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\otimes\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{O}_{X},

whose dual is the de Rham complex for
\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
.

Proof.

By base change it suffices to treat the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+}=\St^{+}
. We have
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
-equivariant de Rham and Koszul complexes

(4.18)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})\otimes\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{\vee,+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{\vee,+})\otimes\St^{\vee}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{\vee,+})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to 0

and

(4.19)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\xrightarrow{d}\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\otimes\St\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to 0

\displaystyle 0\to\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to\cdots\to\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\otimes\St\to\mathring{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St^{+})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to 0.

By the Poincaré lemma for open unit polydiscs
[
Tam15
, Lemma 26]
, the de Rham sequences in both (
4.18
) and (
4.19
) are exact (one can write
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{\vee,+})
as filtered colimit of functions in open unit polydiscs). Moreover, the Koszul resolutions in both equations are duals to the de Rham resolutions via the naive duality between nuclear Fréchet and
LB
spaces of compact type, cf.
[
RJRC22
, Theorem 3.40]
, one deduces that the Koszul resolutions are also exact. Then, the de Rham complex of (
4.18
) is a complex of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{\vee,+})
-comodules proving (1). Similarly, the Koszul resolution of (
4.19
) is also a complex of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+})
-modules obtaining (2).
∎

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}^{+}
be a unitary overconvergent vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There are natural equivalences

\Mod(X/\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})})=\mathrm{CoMod}_{\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})}(\Mod(X))

and

\Mod(X/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}))=\Mod_{\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}(\Mod(X)).

(2)

Consider the maps
X\xrightarrow{f}X/\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}\xrightarrow{g}X
. Then
f
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover and
g
is both weakly cohomologically proper and cohomologically smooth. Moreover, there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]
.

(3)

Consider the maps
X\xrightarrow{f}X/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\xrightarrow{g}X
. Then
f
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover,
g
is cohomologically smooth, and there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[-d]
. Moreover,
g
is co-smooth with proper dual
\mathcal{P}_{g}(1_{X/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})})=1_{X/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}[-2d]
.

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same of Proposition
4.2.5
where Proposition
4.2.4
is replaced by Proposition
4.3.5
, and the de Rham and Koszul resolutions are those of Lemma
4.3.6
.
∎

Theorem 4.3.8

(Cartier duality for open and closed discs)
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There is a natural bi-linear map

F:\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}\times_{X}X/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\to*/\mathbb{G}_{m}

such that
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is an isomorphism in the Lu-Zheng category, considered in
\LZ_{X}(\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})},X/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+}))
. Furthermore, the inverse of
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is naturally isomorphic to
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[-d]
.

(2)

There is a natural bi-linear map

G:\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\times_{X}X/\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}\to*/\mathbb{G}_{m},

such that
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is an isomorphism in the Lu-Zheng category considered in
\LZ_{X}(\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+}),X/\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})})
. Furthermore, the inverse of
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is naturally isomorphic to
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(-1))\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}[d]
.

In particular, we have analogue Fourier-Moukai isomorphisms as in (
4.14
) and (
4.15
). Moreover, the analogues of Proposition
4.2.9
and Corollary
4.2.10
hold.

Proof.

The proof is totally analogue to the proof of Theorem
4.2.7
, we only explain the construction of the vector bundles
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
and
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
. By functoriality we can always reduce to the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\dagger}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+}=\St^{+}
. For
F
, we need to construct a line bundle on
\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}/\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
, where the quotient is for the trivial action. We take
F^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
as the line bundle
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})
endowed with the (left) multiplication map

\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee}).

Similarly,
G^{*}(\mathscr{O}(1))
is the line bundle on
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+})/\overline{\mathbb{V}(\St)}
induced by the localization of
\mathscr{O}(\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+}))
on
\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+})
endowed with the
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\St^{+,\vee})
-comodule structure given by the adjoint of the (left) multiplication map

\mathscr{O}(\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+}))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{O}(\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+}))\to\mathscr{O}(\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\St^{\vee,+})).

Finally, the last statement follows by the analogue computations of Proposition
4.2.9
and Corollary
4.2.10
.
∎

4.3.2.
Cartier duality for analytic vector bundles

We now state a Cartier duality for the analytification of vector bundles. The restriction of the algebraic group
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
to Tate stacks over
R
is represented by its analytification
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}
as an adic space, see Definition
2.7.24
. We call
{\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff^{b}_{R_{{\square}}})_{/[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}]}
the category of
analytic vector bundles of rank
d
, note however that the data of an analytic vector bundle for a Tate stack is the same as the data of an algebraic vector bundle (this is not true for general solid stacks).

Construction 4.3.9
.

Let
X
be an Tate stack over
X
, and let
f:X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}
be an analytic vector bundle of rank
d
. Let
\St
be the standard vector bundle over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}
, then the analytification of the algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}(\St^{\vee})
admits a group action of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}
and defines an analytic space over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}^{\an}
that we denote by
\mathbb{V}(\St)^{\an}
. We let
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}:=f^{*}\mathbb{V}(\St)^{\an}
be the
analytification
of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
. Dually, let
\iota:X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
be the zero section, we let
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}
be the overconvergent neighbourhood of
\iota
, equivalently, we let

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}=\bigcap_{\varepsilon\to\infty}\pi^{\varepsilon}\overline{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}

for any lattice
\mathscr{F}^{+}\subset\mathscr{F}
that exists locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology.

Definition 4.3.10
.

Let
X
be a Tate stack over
R_{{\square}}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
. We let
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
be the algebra over
\mathscr{O}_{X}
defining the analytic space
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}
. Similarly, we let
\mathrm{Sym}^{\an}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
denote the global sections over
X
of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
.

Proposition 4.3.11
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
R_{{\square}}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be an analytic vector bundle over
X
.

(1)

The map
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}\to X
is weakly cohomologically proper.

(2)

The map
f:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}\to X
is cohomologically smooth and there is a natural isomorphism
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
. If in addition
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, then there is a natural isomorphism
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})[-d]
. In particular,
f_{!}f^{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
and the localization of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
in
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
is
f^{!}1_{X}
.

Proof.

Part (1) is clear since
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}
is the relative analytic spectrum of the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
endowed with the induced analytic structure.

For part (2), smoothness of
f
smoothness follows from Proposition
3.6.13
. The computation of
f^{!}1_{X}
and
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
follows the same lines of the proof of Propositions
4.2.4
and
4.2.13
where we use the idempotent
R[T]
-algebra
R\{T^{-1}\}^{\dagger}[T]
instead, namely, the analytification
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}_{R}\subset\mathbb{A}^{1,alg}_{R}
is the complement of the idempotent algebra
R\{T^{-1}\}^{\dagger}[T]
. We leave the details to the reader.
∎

As we saw before, a key point in the construction of the Cartier duality is having available the de Rham and Koszul resolutions:

Lemma 4.3.12
.

Let
X
be an Tate stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
.

(1)

There is a natural de Rham resolution as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
