-comodule given by the complete decreasing filtration

\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}.

(2)

There is a natural Koszul resolution as
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
-module given by the complete increasing filtration

\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{O}_{X},

whose dual is the Rham complex for
\mathrm{Sym}^{\an}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
.

Proof.

The same proof of Lemma
4.3.6
applies; note that the restriction to Tate stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is for the Poincaré lemma to hold.
∎

Theorem 4.3.13

(Analytic Cartier duality for vector bundles)
.

Let
X
be a Tate stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}
be a vector bundle of rank
d
over
X
. Then the analogue of Propositions
4.2.5
and
4.2.9
, Theorem
4.2.7
and Corollary
4.2.10
hold by replacing the following objects:

—

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
for
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}
,

—

\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}
for
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})^{\an}
,

—

\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\St^{\vee}
for
\mathrm{Sym}^{\dagger}_{X}\St^{\vee}
,

—

\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\St)
by
\mathrm{Sym}^{\an}_{X}(\St)
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same lines of the cited references after some minor adaptations, we left the details to the reader.
∎

4.3.3.
Cartier duality for locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundles

We finish this section with a new Cartier duality that is closely related with the theory of solid locally analytic representations of
[
RJRC23
]
. Let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the ring of
p
-adic integers seen as a
p
-adic Lie group, we let
C^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the space of locally analytic functions of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and denote by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}
its analytic spectrum. Let
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}):=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(C^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the locally analytic distribution algebra of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. By a theorem of Amice, the algebra
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is isomorphic to the global sections of the open unit disc centered at
1
, namely
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}:=1+\mathring{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\subset\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
. The algebra
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
can be written as a limit of analytic distribution algebras
\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which are the dual of functions of the rigid group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}+p^{h}\mathring{\mathbb{G}}_{a,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\subset\mathbb{G}^{\an}_{a,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. The rings
\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
correspond to suitable closed overconvergent discs in
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
of radius
p^{-b(h)}
with
b(h)\to 0^{+}
as
h\to\infty
. By
[
RJRC23
, Theorem 4.1.7]
, there is a natural equivalence between the category of solid locally analytic representations of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and that of quasi-coherent shaves on
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
. Moreover, under this equivalence
\mathbb{Z}_{p}+p^{h}\mathring{\mathbb{G}}_{a,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-analytic representations correspond to modules over
\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, which is an idempotent algebra on
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
. Our next goal is to improve this statement to a Cartier duality theorem in a relative setting for a suitable notion of locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundle. To make this concrete we first need a construction.

Lemma 4.3.14
.

There is a natural action
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}\times\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}\to\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
associated to the adjoint of the multiplication map
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,{\square}}}\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
making
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
an
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}
-module. At the level of points this action corresponds to
(a,\chi)\mapsto\chi^{a}
.

Proof.

Let
h>0
and let
\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the
h
-analytic distribution algebra. The multiplication map

\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,{\square}}}\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

has by adjoint a map

\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})).

Since the map
\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is of trace class for
h^{\prime}>h
, the composite

\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}))

factors through
C^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,{\square}}}\mathcal{D}^{h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Taking colimits as
h\to h^{{}^{\prime},-}
, one gets a map

\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to C^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p,{\square}}}\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

One easily checks that this is a morphism of algebras that endows
\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with a
C^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-comodule structure, namely, it is nothing but the orbit map of
\mathcal{D}^{h^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as locally analytic representation. One checks that these maps are compatible for
h^{\prime}>0
, defining the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}
-module structure
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}\times\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}\to\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
as wanted.
∎

Construction 4.3.15
.

We define the category of locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundles of rank
d
to be the slice category
{\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})_{[*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}]}
where
\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
is the analytic group space associated to the Hopf algebra of locally analytic functions of
\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\St^{+}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
be the standard representation over
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
, then the locally analytic Lie group
\St^{+,la}
has a natural action of
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
that defines an analytic space
\mathbb{V}(\St^{+,la})
. For
X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
, with associated vector bundle
\mathscr{F}
induced by
f:X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
, we denote by
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-locally analytic vector bundle.
We let
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la}):=f^{*}\mathbb{V}(\St^{+,la})
be the geometric incarnation of
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
. Let
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
be the
p
-adic generic fiber of the formal multiplicative group at
1
endowed with its
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}
-module structure. We define the dual space of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})
to be
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+,la}):=\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+,la})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{la}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}
.

Definition 4.3.16
.

Let
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
be a locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundle of rank
d
associated to a map
X\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
. We let
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
denote the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra of functions of the space
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})
. We call
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
the algebra of locally analytic functions of
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
. We denote by
\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
the global sections over
X
of the dual space
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
, we call this object the algebra of locally analytic distributions of
\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+,la}
.

Proposition 4.3.17
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
be a locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundle over
X
.

(1)

The map
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})\to X
is weakly cohomologically proper.

(2)

The map
f:\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})\to X
is cohomologically smooth and there are natural isomorphisms
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]
and
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})[-d]
. In particular,
f_{!}f^{!}1_{X}=\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
.

Proof.

Part (1) is clear since
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})
is the relative analytic spectrum of the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
endowed with the induced analytic structure.

For part (2), smoothness of
f
follows from Proposition
3.6.13
. The computation of
f^{!}1_{X}
and
f_{!}1_{\mathring{\mathbb{V}}(\mathscr{F}^{+})}
follows the same lines of the proof of Propositions
4.2.4
and
4.2.13
after we modify the idempotent algebras. For this, let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
be the canonical basis of
\St
. For
i=1,\ldots,d
let
D_{i}:=\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\langle 1/e_{i}\rangle^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\langle\frac{p^{\varepsilon}}{e_{i}}\rangle
. Then
D_{i}
is an idempotent
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-algebra, we let
C
be the
dg
algebra obtained by taking the “union” of
D_{1},\ldots,D_{d}
. Then, the space
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St)
is the open subspace of
\AnSpec\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
obtained as the complement of the idempotent algebra
C
. Therefore, we can write

f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St)}=[\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to C].

An explicit power series computation shows that
f_{!}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St)}
is
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
C^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[-d]
. Let us see that it has the natural comodule action of
C^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For this, it suffices to compute its dual since it is a
LB
space of compact type. But the dual is given by

f_{*}f^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})[d],

this shows that
f^{!}1_{X}=f^{*}\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d]
, and so

f_{*}f^{!}1_{X}=(f_{*}1_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St)})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}[d],

proving that the algebra structure of
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the one arising from
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St)
as wanted.
∎

In order to obtain an analogue of Theorem
4.2.7
, we need to have access to an analogue of the de Rham and Koszul resolutions of
\mathscr{O}_{X}
of Lemma
4.2.3
:

Lemma 4.3.18
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
a locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundle over
X
of rank
d
.

(1)

We have a natural resolution as
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
-comodule given by a decreasing complete filtration

(4.20)

\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee},

whose dual is the Koszul resolution

\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\otimes\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{O}_{X}.

(2)

We have a natural resolution as
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
-module given by an increasing complete filtration

(4.21)

\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F^{+}})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}\to\cdots\to\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})\otimes\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathscr{O}_{X},

whose dual is the Rham complex

\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\otimes\mathscr{F}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the universal case with
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{la}
and
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}=\St^{la}
. Recall that we have an equivalence of Hopf algebras
\mathbb{Z}_{p,{\square}}[\mathbb{Z}_{p}]=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[1-X]]
sending the unit
[1]\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
to
X\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[1-X]]
. Under this equivalence
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
becomes isomorphic to
\varprojlim_{h\to\infty}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle\frac{1-X}{p^{1/h}}\rangle
. One obtains a
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant Koszul resolution induced by left multiplication of
\St
:

0\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St\to\cdots\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes\St\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to 0.

taking duals one gets the resolution (
4.20
). It is clear that this is a resolution as
C^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-comodules and
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-modules.

For part (2) we argue in the similar way, this time knowing that the de Rham complex

0\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes\St^{\vee}\xrightarrow{d}\cdots\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\St^{\vee}\to 0

is exact by the Poincaré Lemma,
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant, and a complex as
\mathcal{D}^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-comodule and
C^{la}(\St,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-module. Note that, after fixing a basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{d}
of
\St
, the resolution (
4.21
) is the Koszul resolution of the regular sequence
(X_{e_{1}}-1,\ldots,X_{e_{d}}-1)
, where
X_{e_{i}}-1
is the projection to the
i
-th component.
∎

Remark 4.3.19
.

The resolution of Lemma
4.3.18
(1) is not the de Rham complex of
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee,+})
. It is actually obtained by the Lazard-Serre resolution of the Iwasawa algebra, see
[
Koh11
, Theorem 4.4]
.

Theorem 4.3.20

(Cartier duality for locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattices)
.

Let
X
be an analytic stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}
be a locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundle over
X
. Then the analogue of Propositions
4.2.5
and
4.2.9
, Theorem
4.2.7
and Corollary
4.2.10
hold after replacing the following objects:

—

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})
for
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{+,la})
,

—

\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})}
for
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+,la})
,

—

\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}
for
\mathrm{Sym}^{la}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
,

—

\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{X}(\mathscr{F})
by
\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathcal{D}}_{X}(\mathscr{F}^{+})
.

Proof.

The same proof of the references applies in this context after making two modifications: the first one is replacing Lemma
4.2.3
for Lemma
4.3.18
. The second is to provide the suitable compactifications of
f:\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\St^{+})\to*
that are used to compute
f_{!}f^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as in Proposition
4.2.4
.
∎

Remark 4.3.21
.

There are at least two different generalizations of the Cartier duality for locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundles. The first consists in taking a finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and considering instead
L
-locally analytic
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-vector bundles; this theory should be a consequence of what we have done previously since we have a fiber sequence of group objects over
L
:

(\Lie\mathfrak{k})^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-la}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}^{la},

where
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\mathbb{Q}_{p}-la}
is the group
\mathcal{O}_{L}
seen as a
p
-adic Lie group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and
\mathfrak{k}=\ker(\Lie_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{L}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}L\to\Lie_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L})
. A different and more interesting generalization is the passage from locally analytic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-vector bundles to locally analytic
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-vector bundles. In this case, the Cartier dual of a locally analytic vector bundle
\mathscr{F}^{la}
should be given by the “
universal cover
”
\widetilde{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})
, where
\widetilde{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee})=\varprojlim_{p}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m,\eta}(\mathscr{F}^{\vee,+})
is the limit as analytic spaces of multiplication by
p
of the dual of a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
\mathscr{F}^{+,la}\subset\mathscr{F}^{la}
(such a lattice exists locally in the
\mathscr{D}
-topology).

5.
Algebraic and analytic de Rham stacks

In this section we construct the algebraic and analytic (filtered) de Rham stacks for derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Following
[
Bha22
]
, we will obtain the Hodge-filtration of the de Rham cohomology by reading the geometry of these stacks. We shall prove that both the algebraic and analytic de Rham stacks have a nice theory of six functors for morphisms locally of solid finite presentation of derived Tate adic spaces. Finally, we compute the dualizing sheaves of both filtered de Rham stacks for solid smooth morphisms by applying a deformation to the normal cone argument as in
[
Man22b
]
and
[
CS22
]
.

5.1.
The algebraic de Rham stack

The (algebraic) de Rham stack was introduced by Simpson
[
Sim96
,
ST97
]
, and plays a fundamental role in the geometric Langlands correspondence, cf.
[
GR14
]
. In the following we will define algebraic de Rham prestacks in the realm of analytic geometry over
\mathbb{Q}
. We then specialize to solid prestacks and show that the theory of
D
-modules obtained in this way admits a good behaved six-functor formalism.

5.1.1.
General definition

Definition 5.1.1
.

Let
\PSh(\AnRing_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\op})
be the category of prestacks on analytic rings over
\mathbb{Q}
. We define the following objects

(1)

Let
X\in\PSh(\AnRing_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\op})
. The
absolute filtered algebraic de Rham prestack of
X
is the prestack of anima over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by

X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}(\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathcal{A})=\varinjlim_{I\to\mathcal{A}}X(\mathrm{cone}(I\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})),

where
I
runs over all the uniformly nilpotent ideals of
\mathcal{A}
, see Definition
2.5.8
. We define the
absolute de Rham (resp. Hodge) prestack

X_{dR}^{\alg}
(resp,
X_{Hodge}^{\alg}
) to be the pullback along
*=\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
(resp. the pullback along
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
). When restricted to solid rings, the
filtered de Rham stack
is the
\mathscr{D}
-sheafification of the filtered de Rham prestack (resp. for the de Rham and the Hodge stacks).

(2)

For a morphism
X\to Y
of prestacks on analytic rings over
\mathbb{Q}
we let
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}
be the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}.}

Similarly, we define
X_{dR,Y}^{\alg}
and
X_{Hodge,Y}^{\alg}
to be the pullback of
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
to
Y
and
Y\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
respectively.

The following proposition describes some basic properties of the algebraic de Rham stack.

Proposition 5.1.2
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks on
\AnRing_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

(1)

Suppose that
f
is formally étale, then the natural map
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}
is an equivalence.

(2)

Suppose that
f
is formally smooth and let
\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}=\AnSpec_{X}\mathrm{Sym}_{X}^{\bullet}\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}
. The following hold

(a)

The map
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}
is an epimorphism.

(b)

There is a natural equivalence
X_{Hodge,Y}^{\alg}=(X\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}}(-1)
.

(3)

The formation of
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
commutes with small colimits and finite limits of prestacks.

Proof.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\AnRing_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and let
\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A}
be a generalized Cartier divisor and
I\to\mathcal{A}
an uniformly nilpotent ideal (cf. Definition
2.5.8
). Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathrm{cone}(I\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})
, then
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a nilpotent thickening and there is a natural equivalence

(5.1)

(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})(\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{\sim}X(\mathcal{B})\times_{Y(\mathcal{B})}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})(\mathcal{A})=(X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\times_{Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))(\mathcal{A})

proving what we wanted.

(2)

It is clear that if
f
is formally smooth then
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is an epimorphism, namely, the map (
5.1
) is surjective by definition of formally smoothness; this shows (a). To prove part (b), note that
\mathcal{A}
-points of
X^{\alg}_{Hodge,Y}
are given by

X^{\alg}_{Hodge,Y}(\mathcal{A})=\varinjlim_{I\subset\mathcal{A}}X(I\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1]\oplus\mathcal{A})\times_{Y(I\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1]\oplus\mathcal{A})}(Y\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m})(\mathcal{A}).

Thus, given
\eta\in Y(\mathcal{A})
, the fiber of
X^{\alg}_{Hodge,Y}\to Y\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
at
\eta\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is given by

\varinjlim_{I\subset\mathcal{A}}\Map_{\Mod_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{A})}(\eta^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X/Y},I\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1])=(\eta^{*}\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{X/Y}\otimes\Nil(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1))[1])(*),

which shows that

X_{Hodge,Y}^{\alg}=(X\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}}(-1).

(3)

Finally, by definition at the level of points,
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
commutes with small colimits. The commutation with finite limits follows from the definition and the fact that the system of uniformly bounded ideals of a ring
\mathcal{A}
is filtered.

∎

Remark 5.1.3
.

There is a different definition for the de algebraic (filtered) de Rham prestack, namely, the prestack given by

X^{alg^{\prime}}_{dR^{+}}(\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathcal{A})=X(\mathrm{cone}(\Nil(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})),

see §
2.5
for the definition of the condensed nil-radical. The apparent advantage of this definition is that the formation of
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+}}^{alg^{\prime}}
commutes with small limits and colimits of prestacks, however, after taking sheafifications with respect to some Grothendieck topology the formation of the de Rham stack will only commutes with small colimits and finite limits. The disadvantage of this definition is that it is not clear whether formally smooth maps produce epimorphisms. Nevertheless, for all the spaces in practice both constructions are the same after a suitable sheafification, eg. smooth morphisms of classical derived schemes (this follows from the fact that the nilpotent radical of a finitely generated algebra is nilpotent), and solid smooth morphisms of derived Tate adic spaces (this follows from Proposition
3.7.5
as
\Nil(\mathcal{A})\subset\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})
for a bounded affinoid ring).

Another feature of the de Rham stack is its relation with formal completions of Zariski closed immersions.

Proposition 5.1.4
.

Let
X=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}\to Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of analytic affine spaces over
\mathbb{Q}
surjective on
\pi_{0}
, and such that
\mathcal{B}
has the induced analytic structure from
\mathcal{A}
(i.e. a Zariski closed immersion). Let
I=[\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}]
and suppose that there is an extremally disconnected set
S
and a map
S\to I
such that
\mathcal{A}[S]\to I
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
. Then the algebraic de Rham stack
X_{dR,Y}^{\alg}
is the full substack of
Y
given by

X_{dR,Y}^{\alg}=\varinjlim_{n}Y\times_{\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]}\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]_{n}^{\mathbb{L}}.

We call
X_{dR,Y}^{\alg}
the formal completion of
Y
along
X
and denote it by
\widehat{Y}^{X}
.

Proof.

Let us write
\widehat{Y}^{X}:=\varinjlim_{n}Y\times_{\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]}\AnSpec\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]_{n}^{\mathbb{L}}
, by Proposition
2.5.7
the map
\widehat{Y}^{X}\to X
is an immersion. Since for any analytic ring
\mathcal{C}
the ideal of the map
\mathcal{C}\to\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C})
is uniformly nilpotent, the algebraic de Rham prestack is the right Kan extension of its restriction to static analytic rings. In particular,
X_{dR}
and
Y_{dR}
are just presheaves on sets and
Y_{dR}\to X_{dR}
is an immersion. Thus, to show that
X_{dR,Y}^{\alg}=\widehat{Y}^{X}
, it suffices to check at the level of points in static analytic rings. Let
J\to\mathcal{C}
be an uniformly nilpotent ideal of an static analytic ring
\mathcal{C}
, suppose we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}/J.}

Let
S\to I
be such that
\mathcal{A}[S]\to I
is surjective, then the image of
S
in
\mathcal{C}
belongs to
J
, and since
J
is uniformly nilpotent there is
n
such that
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}
factors though

\mathcal{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]}\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{N}[S]]_{n}^{\mathbb{L}}\to\mathcal{C},

proving that the map
\AnSpec\mathcal{C}\to Y
factors through
\widehat{Y}^{X}
as wanted.
∎

Remark 5.1.5
.

The hypothesis that
\mathcal{A}[S]\to I
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
can be thought as a finitely generated assumption for the Zariski closed immersion. With the alternative definition of the de Rham stack of Remark
5.1.3
one can extend Proposition
5.1.4
to an arbitrary Zariski closed immersion.

5.1.2.
Six functors for algebraic
D
-modules

Let us now restrict ourselves to the category of solid prestacks over
\mathbb{Q}
, namely, the category
\PSh(\Aff_{\mathbb{Q}})
of presheaves on anima of solid affinoid rings over
\mathbb{Q}
. We can transmute the six functor formalism from quasi-coherent sheaves to
D
-modules as follows:

Definition 5.1.6
.

Let
S\in\PSh(\Aff_{\mathbb{Q}})
be a fixed prestack. We define the six-functor formalisms
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
,
\mathscr{D}_{dR,S}^{\alg}
and
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}^{\alg}
from
\PSh(\Aff_{\mathbb{Q}})_{/S}
to be that obtained via Lemma
3.1.4
and the functors
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
,
X_{dR,S}^{\alg}
and
X_{Hodge,S}
, landing on
\mathscr{D}
-stacks over
S\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
S
and
S\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
respectively. We call
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
(resp.
\mathscr{D}_{dR,S}^{\alg}
, resp.
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}^{\alg}
) the six functor formalisms of filtered algebraic
D
-modules (resp. algebraic
D
-modules, resp. algebraic Hodge modules) over
S
.

By Theorem
3.1.7
we have a six functor formalism on prestacks for the different
\mathscr{D}
-topologies defined by the functors
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
,
\mathscr{D}_{dR,S}^{\alg}
and
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}^{\alg}
. There is no reason for these topologies to be the same, and not even comparable with the
\mathscr{D}
-topology for solid quasi-coherent sheaves. Nevertheless there are some particular cases where one can relate covers in different topologies.

Lemma 5.1.7
.

Let
S
be a base prestack and
X\to Y
a morphism of
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR^{+},S}
-stacks which is a canonical cover and such that
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover. Then
X\to Y
is a
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR^{+},S}
,
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR,S}
and
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}^{\alg}
-cover.

Proof.

Being a
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR^{+},S}
-cover means that it is a canonical cover and of universal
*
and
!
-descent for the six functor formalism
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR^{+},S}(X)=\Mod_{{\square}}(X^{\alg}_{dR^{+},S})
. The map
X\to Y
is a canonical cover by definition, and by hypothesis
X_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+},S}^{\alg}
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover, in particular of universal
*
and
!
-descent. The lemma follows since the formation of the filtered de Rham stack commutes with finite limits (see Proposition
5.1.2
(c) and
[
Lur09
, Remark 6.2.2.11]
), and the fact that
X_{dR,S}^{\alg}
and
X_{Hodge,S}^{\alg}
are the fibers over
\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}
respectively.
∎

Let
R=\mathbb{Z}((\pi))
, our next task is to show that morphisms locally of solid finite presentation of derived Tate adic spaces admit
!
-functors for the six functor formalism of algebraic filtered
D
-modules. Moreover, we prove an analogue existence result for morphisms of locally finite presentation in Berkovich geometry and
\dagger
-geometry. Finally, we show that solid smooth (resp. étale) maps are sent to cohomologically smooth (resp. étale) maps under
\mathscr{D}^{\alg}_{dR}
in characteristic
0
.

Definition 5.1.8
.

(1)

We let
R\langle T\rangle
denote the Tate algebra of
R
endowed with the induced analytic structure from
R
. Given
\mathcal{A}
a bounded affinoid ring over
R
, we let
\mathcal{A}\langle T\rangle:=\mathcal{A}\otimes_{R_{{\square}}}R\langle T\rangle
.

(2)

We let
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}=\varinjlim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}R\langle\pi^{\varepsilon}T\rangle
denote the algebra of overconvergent functions of a closed unit disc. Given
\mathcal{A}
a bounded affinoid ring we let
\mathcal{A}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}:=\mathcal{A}\otimes_{R_{{\square}}}R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
.

Recall the notion of a coordinate theory of Definition
3.3.1
. The natural maps
R[T]\to R\langle T\rangle
and
R[T]\to R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
define two coordinate theories over
R
, this allows us to talk about morphisms locally of finite presentation for
R\langle T\rangle
and
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
, see Definition
3.3.3
.

Definition 5.1.9
.

A Berkovich adic space (resp. a
\dagger
-space) is a
R\langle T\rangle
-adic space (resp. a
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
-adic space) as in Definition
3.3.3
(3). A morphism of Berkovich adic spaces (resp.
\dagger
-adic spaces) is locally of finite presentation if it is locally of
R\langle T\rangle
-finite presentation (resp. of
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
-finite presentation).

Lemma 5.1.10
.

Let
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
denote the analytic spectrum of
\mathbb{Q}[T]
,
\mathbb{Z}[T]_{{\square}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
,
R\langle T\rangle\otimes\mathbb{Q}
or
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Let
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
be the formal completion at
0
of the twisted line bundle
\mathscr{O}(-1)
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and consider the natural morphism of abelian group objects
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The following hold:

(1)

\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}=(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
. In particular the maps

f:*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}=\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}

and

g:\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to*_{dR^{+}}

admit
!
-functors. Furthermore, the following properties are satisfied

(a)

The pullback of
f
to the algebraic de Rham stack is
(-1)
-truncated and cohomologically étale.

(b)

If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}=\AnSpec(\mathbb{Z}[T]_{{\square}}\otimes\mathbb{Q})
, then
g
is cohomologically smooth.

(c)

If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}[T]
,
\AnSpec R\langle T\rangle\otimes\mathbb{Q}
or
\AnSpec R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, then
g
is cohomologically co-smooth.

(2)

Let
n\geq 1
and denote
X_{n}:=\AnSpec(\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}[n]))
. Then

X_{n,dR^{+}}^{\alg}=(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{\times n+1/\mathbb{G}_{a}})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1),

where
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
acts diagonally on the fiber product. In particular, the maps
X_{n,dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
and
*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to X_{n,dR^{+}}^{\alg}
admit
!
-functors. Furthermore, their restriction to the de Rham stack is an equivalence.

Proof.

(1)

By definition,
\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{\alg}
represents the functor

(\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})\mapsto\mathrm{cone}(\Nil(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})(*),

this implies that
\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{\alg}=(\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
. On the other hand, for any other group
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
, the map
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\to\mathbb{G}_{a}
(with
R
-extension of scalars if necessary) is formally étale. Since
\mathbb{G}_{a}
and
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
are formally smooth, by Proposition
5.1.2
(2) we have a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{\alg}}

where the vertical arrows are epimorphisms. One deduces that
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
as wanted.

(a)

The map
f:*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is equivalent to the map

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\to(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1).

since
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
has
!
-functors, one deduces that
f
does so. The restriction to the de Rham stack yields the functor

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a},

which defines an open immersion of locales, so it is cohomologically étale.

(b) and (c)

The map
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
factors as the composite

(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\to(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}.

When
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}
, the first map is smooth by Lemma
3.6.12
and the second is smooth by Proposition
4.2.5
, then so is the composition. If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
is any other coordinate, the first map is weakly cohomologically proper being a quotient of a map of analytic rings with the induced analytic structure, and the second is co-smooth by Proposition
4.2.5
again, then so is the composition.

(2)

Since the filtered algebraic de Rham functor commutes with finite limits, and
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}[n+1])=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbb{Q}[n])}\mathbb{Q}
for
n\geq 0
, we have the desired description of
X_{n,dR^{+}}^{\alg}
. It is also clear that the maps
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
and
*_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
admit
!
-functors from this description as stacks.

∎

Lemma 5.1.11
.

Let
f:X=\AnSpec\mathcal{A}\to Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}
be a morphism of bounded affinoid rings over
(R\otimes\mathbb{Q})^{\an}
.

(1)

If
f
is standard solid smooth (resp. étale) then
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is cohomologically smooth (resp.étale).

(2)

Suppose that
X=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{d}X_{i}
with
X_{i}\to Y
standard solid smooth. If
f
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover then
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover. In particular, solid rational covers of
Y
give rise
\mathscr{D}
-covers of
Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
.

(3)

Suppose that
f:X\to Y
is a rational cover for the coordinate theories
R\langle T\rangle
and
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Then
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover.

Proof.

(1)

We first assume that
\mathbb{L}_{Y}
is a projective
\mathcal{B}
-module. By deformation theory the map
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is surjective, in particular of universal
*
-descent. Then, to show that
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is cohomologically smooth it suffices to show that
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y
is cohomologically smooth. We can factor
X\to Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{n}\xrightarrow{\pr_{Y}}Y
with the first map being standard solid étale. By Lemma
5.1.10
the projection
(Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{n})_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is cohomologically smooth, so we only need to show that the arrow
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}^{n}
is cohomologically étale. But again, the cotangent complex of
Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{n}
is a projective
\mathcal{B}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\rangle_{{\square}}
-module, and by the same argument it suffices to show that if
X\to Y
is standard solid étale then
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is cohomolgically étale. By Proposition
5.1.2
(1) we have that
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}=X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
proving what we wanted. Now we show the general case, since the formation of the filtered de Rham stack commutes with finite limits, it suffices to construct a Cartesian diagram

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime}}

where
X^{\prime}=\AnSpec\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is a standard solid smooth (resp. étale) morphism of bounded affinoid rings (not necessarily over
\mathbb{Q}
!), and
\mathbb{L}_{Y^{\prime}}
a projective
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
-module. Since
\mathcal{B}
is bounded affinoid, we can write
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{B})
as a filtered colimit of quotients of rings of the form
R\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]
where
\underline{X}
is a finite set of variables and
K
is a profinite set. Writing
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f_{1},\ldots,f_{c})
as a standard solid smooth map, we can find a map
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}:=R\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle\to\mathcal{A}
and a lift
f_{i}^{\prime}
of
f_{i}
to
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}
such that
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle_{{\square}}/^{\mathbb{L}}(f^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,f^{\prime}_{c})
is standard solid smooth. Taking
Y^{\prime}=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime}=\AnSpec\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
we get the desired cartesian diagram, namely, the cotangent complex of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}[K]
with
\underline{X}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{k})
.

(2)

By Proposition
3.1.15
, it suffices to show that the pullback along the map
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is conservative, for this it suffices to see that it is a surjection as
\mathscr{D}
-stacks. Suppose first that
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is surjective as
\mathscr{D}
-stacks, in particular of universal
*
-descent. Then, it suffices to show that the map
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is surjective, but we have a factorization
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Since
X\to Y
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover we deduce that
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is surjective as wanted. In general, consider the topology
\mathscr{T}
on
\Aff^{b}_{R\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
with covers given by solid smooth morphisms that are
\mathscr{D}
-covers. We proved that the formation of the filtered de Rham stack satisfies
\mathscr{T}
-descent for covers
U\to W
such that
W\to W_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is surjective. To show descent for a general morphism it suffices to construct a Cartesian diagram as in (
5.2
) such that
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime},\alg}
are surjective as morphisms of
\mathscr{D}
-stacks. By part (1) we can find a cartesian diagram

(5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y^{\prime\prime}}

of bounded affinoid rings where
\mathbb{L}_{Y^{\prime\prime}}
is a projective module in
\Mod_{{\square}}(Y^{\prime\prime})
(even compact projective), in particular
Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime\prime},\alg}
is surjective. However,
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
might not be a surjection. Let
Y^{\prime}\subset Y^{\prime\prime}
be the full
\mathscr{T}
-substack consisting in the essential image of
X^{\prime}
in
Y^{\prime\prime}
, concretely, it is the geometric realization of the simplicial
\mathscr{T}
-stack
(X^{{}^{\prime}n+1/Y^{\prime\prime}})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
. Then, since
X\to Y
is a
\mathscr{T}
-cover, we still have a Cartesian diagram

\hbox to82.17pt{\vbox to47.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 41.08325pt\lower-23.8373pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {
{}{}{}}{}{
