{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -18.99 -24.46 L 14.55 -24.46}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.71805pt}{-17.67757pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

On the other hand, since
Y^{\prime}\subset Y^{\prime\prime}
is a full
\mathscr{T}
-substack, the morphism
Y^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime},\alg}
is surjective as
\mathscr{D}
-stacks, namely, it is the geometric realization of the surjective morphism of simplicial
\mathscr{D}
-stacks

(X^{{}^{\prime}n+1/Y^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}\to(X^{{}^{\prime}n+1/Y^{\prime\prime},\alg}_{dR^{+}})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}.

The lemma follows.

(3)

For part (3), denote
R(T)
for
R\langle T\rangle
or
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
, and let us write
f:X=\bigsqcup X_{i}\to Y
with
X_{i}\to Y
a
R(T)
-rational localization. In particular, each map
X_{i}\to Y
is defined by an idempotent
\mathscr{O}_{Y}
-algebra and defines a closed subset of the locale of
Y
. Suppose first that
Y\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is surjective, then since
X_{i}\to Y
is formally étale (cf. Example
3.4.10
), we have a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{i,dR^{+}}^{\alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}}

where the vertical arrows are epimorphisms. Then, the inclusion
X_{i,dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is defined by an idempotent algebra in
\Mod_{{\square}}(Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg})
, and defines a closed subspace of the locale of
Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
. Since
X\to Y
is a cover, we have
\bigcup_{i}X_{i}=Y
as closed subspaces of the locale, which implies that
\bigcup_{i}X_{i,dR^{+}}^{\alg}=Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
. Since there are only finitely many
i
’s, one deduces that the map
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is descendable, and a
\mathscr{D}
-cover by Proposition
3.1.16
.

Let us now deal with the general case. As in part (1), we can assume that there is a bounded affinoid space
Y^{\prime\prime}=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
such that
Y^{\prime\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime\prime},\alg}
is surjective, a morphism
X^{\prime}=\bigsqcup_{i}X_{i}^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
consisting in finitely many
R(T)
-rational localizations (that might not cover
Y^{\prime\prime}
), and a cartesian square as in (
5.3
). Then, taking
Y^{\prime}\subset Y
to be the union of the
X_{i}^{\prime}
in
Y^{\prime\prime}
, we have a Cartesian diagram as in (
5.2
) where
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
and a descendable morphism. By the first case treated we get a descendable morphism
X_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime},\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{{}^{\prime},\alg}
whose pullback to
Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
gives rise a descendable morphism
X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
, in particular a
\mathscr{D}
-cover as wanted.

∎

Theorem 5.1.12

(Six functors for algebraic
D
-modules)
.

Let us write
R(T)
for
R\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
,
R\langle T\rangle
and
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
R\otimes\mathbb{Q}
locally of
R(T)
-finite presentation, and let
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}:X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
be the associated morphism of algebraic filtered de Rham stacks. Then
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
admits
!
-functors. Furthermore, if
f
is solid smooth (resp. solid étale), then
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is cohomologically smooth (resp. cohomologically étale). Moreover, the formation of the filtered de Rham stack satisfies descent for solid smooth covers, namely, solid smooth maps
f
such that
f^{*}
is conservative (cf. Proposition
3.1.15
).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1.11
(2) and (3), the formation of the de Rham stack satisfies analytic
R(T)
-descent for
R(T)=R\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
,
R\langle T\rangle
and
R\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Then the existence of
!
-functors for morphisms locally of
R(T)
-finite presentation follows from Lemma
5.1.10
and Proposition
3.3.6
. The fact that solid smooth maps, resp. étale maps, resp. analytic open subspaces are sent to cohomologically smooth maps, resp. cohomologically étale maps, resp. open immersions of locales, follows from Lemma
5.1.11
(1) and the fact that the formation of the filtered de Rham stack commutes with finite limits. Finally, descent for solid smooth covers follows from Lemma
5.1.11
(2).
∎

5.1.3.
Hodge filtration of the de Rham cohomology

We finish this section with the construction of the Hodge filtration of the compactly supported de Rham cohomology of a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
R\otimes\mathbb{Q}
, this discussion follows closely
[
Bha22
, §2]
.

Theorem 5.1.13
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
R\otimes\mathbb{Q}
of relative dimension
d
, and let
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
be the associated map of filtered de Rham stacks. Then the compactly supported de Rham cohomology

DR_{c}(X/Y):=f_{dR^{+},!}^{alg}f_{dR^{+}}^{!}1_{Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}}

is complete with respect to its natural filtration. Moreover, we have a Hodge filtration

\gr^{-i}(DR_{c}(X/Y))\cong f_{!}\Omega^{i,\vee}_{X/Y}(-i)[i].

when pullbacked to an object in
Y
.

Proof.

In order to see that
DR_{c}(X/Y)
is complete, we can work locally in the analytic topology of
X
and
Y
, and assume that both are affinoid and
f
is standard solid smooth. Furthermore, by taking a cartesian square (
5.2
) as in the proof of Lemma
5.1.11
, and proper base change, we can assume that
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
is surjective. Thus, to prove that
DR_{c}(X/Y)
is complete, it suffices to consider its pullback to
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, or equivalently, take the cohomology with compact support of the map
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. On the other hand, we have a factorization

X\to Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}\to Y

which gives rise to a factorization

X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}\xrightarrow{h}Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}^{alg,d}\xrightarrow{g}Y\times B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{d}\xrightarrow{k}Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}.

Note that we have a diagram with cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\alg}_{dR^{+},Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}^{alg,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{d}.}

By Theorem
3.6.15
and Proposition
4.2.5
we have an (a priori non-natural) equivalence

f_{dR^{+},Y}^{!}1_{Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\simeq h^{*}g^{!}k^{!}1_{Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\simeq 1_{X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}}(-d),

in particular it is filtered complete, and then so is
h_{!}f_{dR^{+},Y}^{!}1_{Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
. One deduces completeness for
DR_{c}(X/Y)
from the following lemma:

Lemma 5.1.14
.

Let
g:Y\times B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{d}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. For any filtered complete module
\mathcal{F}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(Y\times B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{d})
,
g_{!}\mathcal{F}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
is also filtered complete.

Proof.

By Cartier duality Theorem
4.2.7
, we have a natural
\Mod_{{\square}}(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
-linear equivalence of categories of solid quasi-coherent sheaves

FM^{-1}:\Mod_{{\square}}(Y\times B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)^{d})\cong\Mod_{{\square}}(Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a}(1)^{d}).

Let
\iota:Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a}(1)^{d}
be the zero section, then Corollary
4.2.10
(ii) implies that

g_{!}\mathcal{F}=\iota^{*}FM^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\otimes(g^{!}1_{Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{-1})=\iota^{*}FM^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathscr{O}(d)[d]).

But the object
\iota^{*}FM^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathscr{O}(d)[d])
has a finite Koszul filtration with graded pieces given by twists of finite direct sums of
FM^{-1}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathscr{O}(d)[d])
, proving that it is still complete.
∎

It is left to compute the graded pieces of
DR_{c}(X/Y)
, this follows from Proposition
4.2.5
and that
X_{Hodge,Y}^{\alg}=(X\times B\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{X/Y}(-1)
by Proposition
5.1.2
(2.b).
∎

Remark 5.1.15
.

Theorem
5.1.13
was stated for de Rham cohomology with compact supports due to its well behaviour with respect to the six functors. One recovers the completeness and Hodge filtration of the usual de Rham cohomology by taking duals. Moreover, the fact that the cohomology of the de Rham stack coincides with the hypercohomology of the de Rham complex for classical smooth morphisms of rigid spaces follows by the same argument as in
[
Bha22
, Theorem 2.3.6]
by reduction to the case of the unit disc, we left the details to the reader.

5.2.
The analytic de Rham stack

In Section
5.1
we introduced the algebraic filtered de Rham stack and proved that it has a reasonable theory of six functors for derived Tate adic spaces. In the next section we will introduce a variant of this construction that for our convenience we specialize to Tate stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The new theory of
D
-modules obtained from this stack is an enhancement of the theory of
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules of Ardakov and Wadsley
[
AW19
,
AW18
]
, that we call analytic
D
-modules. A more concrete comparison between analytic
D
-modules and Ardakov and Wadsley’s
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules is left to a future work. For example, for a smooth rigid space
X
, we expect coadmissible
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules to be precisely the smooth objects on
X_{dR}
. Instead, we shall construct a six functor formalism for analytic
D
-modules, and prove good cohomological properties for morphisms of solid finite presentation. Once the relation between analytic
D
-modules and
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules is made, the six functors constructed hereby will give a very large extension of the six functors of Bode in
[
Bod21
]
.

5.2.1.
Construction of the stacks

Let
\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
be the category of bounded affinoid analytic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
\PSh(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the category of prestacks on
\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
{\operatorname{Sh}}_{\mathscr{D}}(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
the category of Tate stacks over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Remark 5.2.1
.

The functor
\Aff^{b,\op}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\Ani
sending
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\mathcal{A}(*)
is not longer represented by the algebraic affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, instead, it is represented by its analytification
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}
as a rigid space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Similarly, the functor
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\mathcal{A}(*)^{\times}
of units is represented by the analytification of the multiplicative group
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
.

Definition 5.2.2
.

We define the following objects

(1)

Let
X\in\PSh(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. The
absolute filtered analytic de Rham prestack of
X
is the prestack over
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
defined as

X_{dR^{+}}(\mathscr{O}(1)\to\mathcal{A})=X(\cone[\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A}]).

The
absolute analytic de Rham prestack
X_{dR}
(resp. the absolute analytic Hodge prestack
X_{Hodge}
)
is the pullback of
X_{dR^{+}}
to
*=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
(resp. the pullback to
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
). The
\mathscr{D}
-sheafification of
X_{dR^{+}}
is called the filtered analytic de Rham stack and denoted in the same way (similarly for
X_{dR}
and
X_{Hodge}
).

(2)

Let
S\in\PSh(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and let
X
be prestack over
S
. The
relative filtered analytic de Rham prestack of
X
over
S
is the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{dR^{+}}.}

The
relative analytic de Rham and Hodge prestacks
are defined as the pullback of
X_{S,dR^{+}}
to
*=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
and
*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
respectively. If
S
is a Tate stack the relative filtered analytic de Rham stack of
X
over
S
is the
\mathscr{D}
-sheafification of
X_{dR^{+},S}
that denote it in the same way. We define in the obvious way the relative analytic de Rham and Hodge stacks.

Next, we prove some formal properties of the analytic de Rham stack that are deduced from the definition, cf. Proposition
5.1.2
.

Proposition 5.2.3
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of prestacks on
\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

(1)

Suppose that
f
is
\dagger
-formally étale, then the natural map
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
is an equivalence.

(2)

Suppose that
f
is
\dagger
-formally smooth and let
\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}^{\an}=(\AnSpec_{X}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{X}\mathbb{L}_{X})^{\an}
. The following hold

(a)

The map
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
is an epimorphism.

(b)

There is a natural equivalence
X_{Hodge,Y}=(X\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}^{\dagger}(-1)
.

(3)

The formation of
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+}}
commutes with small colimits and limits of prestacks.

Proof.

(1)

This follows from the notion of
\dagger
-formally étaleness, cf. Definition
3.7.2
.

(2)

Part (a) follows from the notion of
\dagger
-formally smoothness. For part (b), let
\mathcal{A}\in\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, let
\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A}
be a generalized Cartier divisor and let
\eta\in Y(\mathcal{A})
. Then the fiber of
X_{Hodge,Y}
over
\eta
is given by the fiber product of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta,\mathscr{O}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(\mathcal{A})\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1]\oplus\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y(\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1]\oplus\mathcal{A})}

which is represented by

\Map_{\Mod_{\geq 0}(\mathcal{A})}(\eta^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X/Y},\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)[1])=\eta^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}^{\vee}\otimes\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})(-1)(*)[1].

This shows that
X_{Hodge,Y}=(X\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}^{\dagger}(-1)
as wanted.

(3)

This follows immediately from the definition of the filtered analytic de Rham prestack, as limits and colimits of prestacks are computed at the level of points.

∎

Similarly as for the algebraic de Rham stack, there is a notion of
\dagger
-formal completion or
\dagger
-neighbourhood for a Zariski closed immersion.

Proposition 5.2.4
.

Let
X=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}\to Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{A}
be a morphism of bounded derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
which is surjective on
\pi_{0}
and that has the induced analytic structure (i.e. a Zariski closed immersion). Let
\mathcal{A}^{\dagger/\mathcal{B}}
be the idempotent
\mathcal{A}
-algebra associated to the closed subspace
\Spa^{\dagger}\mathcal{B}\subset\Spa^{\dagger}\mathcal{A}
of Proposition
2.7.19
. Then there is a natural equivalence

X_{dR,Y}\cong\AnSpec\mathcal{A}^{\dagger/\mathcal{B}}.

Proof.

By the proof of Proposition
2.7.19
the map
\mathcal{A}^{\dagger/\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{B}
induces an equivalence in
\dagger
-reduced algebras. This gives rise an equivalence
\AnSpec(\mathcal{A}^{\dagger/\mathcal{B}})|_{\Aff^{b,\dagger-\red}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=\AnSpec(\mathcal{B})|_{\Aff^{b,\dagger-\red}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
. But by definition,
X_{dR}
is the right Kan extension of the restriction of
X
to
\Aff^{b,\dagger-\red}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. The proposition follows since
X_{dR}\to Y_{dR}
is an immersion, and
X_{dR,Y}\subset Y
is the full sub prestack mapping to
X_{dR}
.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain Kashiwara equivalence for analytic
D
-modules and Zariski closed immersions.

Corollary 5.2.5

(Kashiwara equivalence)
.

Let
X\to Y
be a Zariski closed immersion of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
Y^{\dagger/X}\subset X
be the overconvergent neighbouhood of
X
in
Y
obtained by gluing the rings
\mathcal{B}^{\dagger/\mathcal{A}}
of Proposition
5.2.4
in the analytic topology of
X
. Then there is a natural equivalence of analytic de Rham stacks
X_{dR}=Y^{\dagger/X}_{dR}
. In particular, the category of analytic
D
-modules of
Y
supported on
X
is equivalent to the category of analytic
D
-modules of
X
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2.9
down below the formation of the analytic de Rham stacks satisfies descent for the analytic topology. Then, it suffices to prove the statement in the affinoid case of Proposition
5.2.4
. But the analytic de Rham stack of
X
if the
\mathscr{D}
-sheafification of the right Kan extension of the restriction of
X
to
\Aff^{b,\dagger-\red}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and the
\dagger
-reductions of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\dagger/\mathcal{A}}
are isomorphic by construction. This proves the corollary.
∎

5.2.2.
Six functor formalism for analytic
D
-modules

Definition 5.2.6
.

Let
S\in\PSh(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
. We define the six functor formalisms
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}
,
\mathscr{D}_{dR,S}
and
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}
for
\PSh(\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})_{/S}
to be the six functor formalism obtained by Lemma
3.1.4
applied to the functors
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+},S}
,
X_{dR,S}
and
X_{Hodge,S}
, landing in
\mathscr{D}
-stacks over
S\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
,
S
and
S\times*/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
respectively. The six functor formalism
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}
(resp.
\mathscr{D}_{dR,S}
, resp.
\mathscr{D}_{Hodge,S}
) is called the six functor formalism of filtered analytic
D
-modules over
S
(resp. of analytic
D
-modules over
S
, resp. of Hodge modules over
S
).

Remark 5.2.7
.

The analogue of Lemma
5.1.7
holds for the analytic de Rham stack, namely, if
X\to Y
is a canonical cover such that
X_{dR^{+},S}\to Y_{dR^{+},S}
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover, then
X\to Y
is a
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+},S}
-cover.

Our next task is to show that the six functor formalism of filtered analytic
D
-modules admits
!
-functors for morphisms locally of solid finite presentation of derived Tate adic spaces. We will even prove the existence of
!
-functors for morphisms locally of finite presentation of Berkovich or
\dagger
-adic spaces, see Definition
5.1.9
. Finally, we will show that solid smooth and étale morphisms of derived Tate adic spaces give rise to cohomologically smooth and étale maps at the level of filtered analytic de Rham stacks.

Lemma 5.2.8
.

Let
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
denote the analytic spectrum of one of the algebras
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Let
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
be the line bundle obtained by the analytic spectrum of
\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}(\mathscr{O}(1))
, and let
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}
be its overconvergent neighbourhood at the zero section; we have a natural morphism of group objects
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
. The following hold:

(1)

\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}
. In particular, the maps

f:*_{dR^{+}}=\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}

and

g:\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}\to*_{dR^{+}}

admit
!
-functors. Furthermore, the following properties are satisfies:

(a)

f
is always weakly cohomologically proper. Moreover, its pullback to the de Rham stack is
(-1)
-truncated so cohomologically proper.

(b)

If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
, then
g
is a cohomologically smooth map.

(c)

If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
or
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
, then
g
is weakly cohomologically proper. Furthermore, its pullback to the de Rham stack is
0
-truncated so cohomologically proper,

(2)

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer and denote
X_{n}=\AnSpec\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\bullet}\mathbb{Q}_{p}[n]
. Then

X_{n,dR^{+}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger,\times_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}n+1})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger},

where
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}
acts diagonally. In particular,
X_{dR^{+}}\to*_{dR^{+}}
and
*_{dR^{+}}\to X_{dR^{+}}
admit
!
-functors and are weakly cohomologically proper. Furthermore, their restriction to the de Rham stack is an equivalence.

Proof.

(1)

Let
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\an}=\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}
be the analytic affine line seen as an additive group. By definition,
\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{\an}
represents the functor on bounded affinoid algebras over
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by

(\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})\mapsto\cone(\Nil^{\dagger}\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})(*).

For
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}
, since the image of
\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)
in
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A})
is
\dagger
-nilpotent, by Proposition
2.6.16
the sub prestack
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}\subset\mathbb{G}_{a,dR}
consists on the functor

(\mathscr{O}(-1)\to\mathcal{A})\mapsto\cone(\Nil^{\dagger}(\mathcal{A})\otimes\mathscr{O}(-1)(*)\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}(\mathcal{A})).

This shows that
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}
is represented by the stack
(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}
as wanted.

(a)

The map
*_{dR^{+}}\to\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}
is equivalent to the morphism

\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}/(\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\to(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an})/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}.

This map admits
!
-functors and is weakly cohomologically proper since
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\to(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})
has the induced analytic structure. Furthermore, its pullback to the analytic de Rham stack is an immersion so
(-1)
-truncated.

(b) and (c)

We have factorizations for
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star,dR^{+}}\to*_{dR^{+}}

(\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\to(\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}.

If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}
, the first map is cohomologically smooth by Theorem
3.6.15
, and the second is cohomologically smooth by Theorem
4.3.13
and Proposition
4.2.5
, so the composite is also cohomologically smooth. If
\mathbb{G}_{a,\star}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
or
\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
, then the first map is weakly cohomologically proper since it has the induced analytic structure, and the second is weakly cohomologically proper by Theorem
4.3.13
and Proposition
4.2.5
, thus, the composite is weakly cohomologically proper proving what we wanted. It is clear that its pullback to the the Rham stack is
0
=truncated.

(2)

By Proposition
5.2.3
(3), the formation
X\mapsto X_{dR^{+}}
commutes with all small limits and colimits. Then, since
X_{n+1}=*\times_{X_{n}}*
, an inductive argument gives the desired description of
X_{n,dR^{+}}
. The fact that
*_{dR^{+}}\to X_{n,dR^{+}}
and
X_{n,dR^{+}}\to*_{dR^{+}}
admit
!
-functors and that they are weakly cohomologically proper follows a similar argument as part (1). Finally, since the
\dagger
-reduction of
X_{n}
is
*
, they give rise to the sane analytic de Rham stack.

∎

We now prove an analogue of Lemma
5.1.11
.

Lemma 5.2.9
.

Let
f:X\to\AnSpec\mathcal{A}\to Y\AnSpec\mathcal{B}
be a morphism of bounded affinoid rings over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

(1)

If
f
is standard solid smooth (resp. standard solid étale) then
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is cohomologically smooth (resp. étale).

(2)

Suppose that
X=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{d}X_{i}
with
X_{i}\to Y
standard solid smooth. If
f
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover, then
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover.

(3)

Suppose that
f:X\to Y
is a rational cover for the coordinate theories
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Then
X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover.

Proof.

The proof is virtually the same of Lemma
5.1.11
; the only key step is to have generators
\mathcal{B}
of
\AffRing^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is surjective as
\mathscr{D}
-stacks with
Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}
. For this, we can take affinoid spaces of the form
Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}
with
\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle\underline{X}\rangle_{{\square}}\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle
, where
\underline{X}
is a finite set of variables and
K
is a profinite set. Then, the surjection of
Y\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is a consequence of Proposition
2.6.16
.
∎

Theorem 5.2.10

(Six functors for analytic
D
-modules)
.

Let us write
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(T)
for
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle_{{\square}}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
. Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
locally of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(T)
-finite presentation, and let
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
be the associated morphism of algebraic filtered de Rham stacks. Then
f_{dR^{+}}
admits
!
-functors. Furthermore, if
f
is solid smooth (resp. solid étale), then
f_{dR^{+}}
is cohomologically smooth (resp. cohomologically étale). Moreover, the formation of the filtered de Rham stack satisfies descent for solid smooth covers, namely, solid smooth maps
f
such that
f^{*}
is conservative (cf. Proposition
3.1.15
). Finally, if
f
is a qcqs morphism of finite presentation of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle T\rangle^{\dagger}
-adic spaces, the map
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
is co-smooth.

Proof.

This follows the same argument of Theorem
5.1.12
, by replacing Lemmas
5.1.10
and
5.1.11
with Lemmas
5.2.8
and
5.2.9
respectively. For the last statement about qcqs morphisms of finite presentation, by Lemma
5.2.9
(3) it suffices to prove the claim when
f
is a morphism of finite presentation of affinoid rings, this case follows from Lemma
5.2.8
and an inductive argument.
∎

5.2.3.
Comparison with algebraic
D
-modules

Let
X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Consider the relative algebraic de Rham stack
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}
and let
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}
be its pullback to
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, or equivalently, its restriction to
\Aff^{b}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. The definition at the level of points yields a natural map of de Rham stacks
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
. In the following paragraph we will show that the category of analytic
D
-modules of
X
over
Y
embeds fully faithful in the category of algebraic
D
-modules of
X
over
Y
. We will also deduce that the de Rham cohomology is the same when computed with the algebraic or analytic de Rham stacks.

Proposition 5.2.11
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then the natural map
g:X_{dR^{+},Y}^{alg^{\prime}}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
is cohomologically co-smooth. Furthermore, the natural map

(5.4)

1_{X_{dR^{+},Y}}\to g_{*}1_{X_{dR^{+},Y}}

is an equivalence. In particular,
g^{*}:\Mod_{{\square}}(X_{dR^{+},Y})\to\Mod_{{\square}}(X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg,^{\prime}})
is a fully faithful embedding.

Proof.

Both statements are local in the analytic topology of
X
and
Y
, hence we can assume that
f
is a standard solid smooth morphism of bounded affinoid spaces. Both claims are also preserved by base change on
Y
, so we can assume without loss of generality that
Y=\AnSpec\mathcal{B}
with
\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}\langle\underline{X}\rangle\langle\mathbb{N}[K]\rangle
, with
X
a finite set of variables and
K
a profinite set. We can factor
X\to Z\to Y
where
X\to Z
is standard solid étale and
Z=Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}
. We then have Cartesian diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{dR^{+},Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}.}

Since
Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to Z_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}
and
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}\to X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}
are surjective, we have that
X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}=X_{dR^{+},Y}\times_{Z_{dR^{+},Y}}Z_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}
. Thus, by proper base change, we are reduced to consider the case of
X=Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{n}
. By proper base change, the statement is also stable under fiber products over
Y
, so it suffices to consider
X=Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}
, and by base change assume that
Y=*
. By Lemmas
5.1.10
and
5.2.8
we have the explicit descriptions

\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}^{\alg^{\prime}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)

and

\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}=(\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}.

Consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left((\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\right)/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square},dR^{+}}^{\alg^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}.dR^{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

where
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)
acts on the fiber product diagonally. Then, to show that
g
is co-smooth and that (
5.4
) is an equivalence, it suffices to prove the analogue statements for the map
h
. We have an equivalence

\left((\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}\mathbb{G}(-1)^{\dagger}\right)/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)\cong\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1)

induced from the action map by translations
\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}}\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\to(\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Under this equivalence, the action map becomes the projection

\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}\times(\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1))\to\mathbb{G}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}.

Thus, by base change, we are reduce to prove the claims for the map

(\mathbb{G}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}(-1))\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}.

This follows from the following lemma

Lemma 5.2.12
.

Let
X
be an analytic
\mathscr{D}
-stack over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathscr{F}
a vector bundle over
X
of rank
d
. Consider the quotient
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
where
\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}
acts by translations. Then the morphism

g:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger,\alg}_{dR,X}=\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}\to X

is co-smooth and the natural map

1_{X}\to g_{*}1_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By proper base change we can reduce to the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
. Furthermore, since
*\to*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
is surjective, it suffices to prove the claim after taking pullbacks to
*
. Then
\St\cong\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{d}
and since the claim holds after finite fiber products, it suffices to consider the case of
h:\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to*
. But the map
h
factors as
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to*/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\to*
, the first arrow is weakly cohomologically proper since it has the induced analytic structure, and the second is co-smooth by Proposition
4.2.5
; one deduces that
h
is itself co-smooth. Finally,
g_{*}1_{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{a}}
is nothing but the de Rham cohomology of
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}
which is equal to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
by the Poincaré lemma.
∎

∎

Corollary 5.2.13
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces, and let
f_{dR^{+},Y}:X_{dR^{+},Y}\to Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an}
. Then
f_{dR^{+},Y,*}1_{X_{dR^{+},Y}}
is filtered complete and equal to the de Rham cohomology
DR(X/Y)
, namely, the dual of the de Rham cohomology with compact supports of Theorem
5.1.13
(see Remark
5.1.15
).

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR^{+},Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{dR^{+},Y}^{\alg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{dR^{+},Y}}

Then

DR(X/Y)=f_{dR^{+},Y,*}^{\alg}1=f_{dR^{+},Y,*}h_{*}1=f_{dR^{+},Y,*}1.

∎

5.3.
Poincaré duality for
D
-modules

Next, we prove Poincaré duality for filtered algebraic and analytic
D
-modules. The strategy is similar as for coherent cohomology by taking the deformation to the normal cone. We shall adapt
[
Zav23
, §4]
to derived Tate adic spaces.

Definition 5.3.1

(
[
Zav23
, Definition 4.2.1]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be the category of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. A six functor formalism
\mathscr{D}
on
\mathscr{C}
is
premotivic
if the following hold:

(1)

It is
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}
-acyclic, i.e., if we denote
f:\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}\to*
, then the natural map
1\to f_{*}1_{\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}}
is an equivalence in
\mathscr{D}(*)
.

(2)

Any any solid smooth morphism
f:X\to Y
is cohomologically smooth with respect to
\mathscr{D}
.

Remark 5.3.2
.

Theorems
5.1.12
and
5.2.10
imply that solid smooth maps are cohomologically smooth for the six functors
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
and
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+}}
. Furthermore, de Rham cohomology of the analytic affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}
is trivial, by Theorem
5.1.13
and Corollary
5.2.13
we deduce that both
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
and
\mathscr{D}_{dR^{+}}
are motivic.

For a symmetric monoidal category
\mathscr{E}
let
\Pic(\mathscr{E})
denote the full subcategory consisting on invertible objects.

Lemma 5.3.3

(
[
Zav23
, Lemma 2.1.11]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{D}
be a premotivic six functor formalism on
\mathscr{C}
,
X\in\mathscr{C}
and
f:X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}\to X
. Then the pullback functor

f^{*}:\Pic(\mathscr{D}(X))\to\Pic(\mathscr{D}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}))

is fully faithful.

Proof.

The same proof of
loc. cit.
applies.
∎

Definition 5.3.4
.

Let
\mathscr{D}
be a six functor formalism on
\mathscr{C}
. Let
f:X\to Z
be a solid smooth morphism and let
s:Z\to X
be a Zariski closed immersion.

(1)

We denote
C(f,s):=s^{*}f^{!}1_{Z}\in\mathscr{D}(Z)
.

(2)

For a vector bundle
\mathscr{F}
over
X
with projection
f:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}\to X
and zero section
s:X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
, we let
C_{X}(\mathscr{F}):=C(f,s)
.

(3)

Suppose that
\mathscr{O}_{Z}
is a perfect
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module locally in the analytic topology. By Remark
3.6.16
we can form the deformation to the normal cone

Z\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\widetilde{X}\to Z\times\mathbb{P}^{1}

living over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We let
D_{Z}(X)
denote the pullback of
\widetilde{X}
to
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\backslash\{\infty\}
; we get maps

Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{s}}D_{Z}(X)\xrightarrow{\widetilde{f}}Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}.

Proposition 5.3.5

(
[
Zav23
, Proposition 4.2.6]
)
.

Suppose that the six functor formalism
\mathscr{D}
over
\mathscr{C}
is premotivic. Let
f:X\to Z
be a solid smooth morphism with section
s:Z\to X
such that
\mathscr{O}_{Z}
is a perfect
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module locally in the analytic topology. Then, in the notation of Definition
5.3.4
, the object

\widetilde{s}^{*}\widetilde{f}^{!}1_{Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}}\in\Pic(\mathscr{D}(Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}))

lies in the essential image of
\Pic(\mathscr{D}(Z)
).

Proof.

We perform the same series of reductions as in the proof of Theorem
3.6.15
. In fact, we can assume that
Z
is affinoid and replace
X
by an open neighbourhood of the section of
X
. We can then assume that
X\to Z
is standard solid smooth and that we have a standard solid étale map
X\to Z\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}
. By further refining
Z
, we can even assume that the pullback of
Z\to X\to Z\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}
along
X\to Z\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}
is
Z
itself, and reduce to the case where
X=Z\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}
, see the proof of Theorem
3.6.15
. By a change of coordinates, we can suppose that
Z\to X
is the zero section, and by base change that
Z=*
. This last case is covered in Step 3 of
[
Zav23
, Proposition 4.2.6]
.
∎

Corollary 5.3.6

(
[
Zav23
, Corollary 4.2.7 and Theorem 4.2.8]
)
.

In the notation of the Proposition
5.3.5
, let
\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\an}
denote the analytification of the normal cone of
Z
in
X
. There is a natural equivalence

C(f,s)\cong C_{Z}(\mathcal{N}_{X/Y}^{\an}).

Moreover, if
f:X\to Y
is a solid smooth morphism, there is a natural equivalence

f^{!}1_{Y}\cong C_{X}(\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}^{\an})\in\mathscr{D}(X),

where
\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}^{\an}
is the analytification of the tangent space of
X
over
Y
.

Proof.

The same proof of
loc. cit.
applies.
∎

Theorem 5.3.7

(Poincaré duality for
D
-modules)
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces of relative dimension
d
, and let
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}:X_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}\to Y_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
and
f_{dR^{+}}:X_{dR^{+}}\to Y_{dR^{+}}
be the associated maps of stacks. Then there are natural equivalences

f^{alg,!}_{dR^{+}}1=\mathscr{O}(-d).

and

f_{dR^{+}}^{!}1=\mathscr{O}(-d)[2d].

Proof.

By Corollary
5.3.6
it suffices to prove the theorem for a vector bundle
\mathscr{F}
over
X
. By further reducing to the universal case
X=*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
and
\mathscr{F}=\St
, it suffices to prove it for the relative filtered de Rham stacks of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
over
X
. Let
f:\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}\to X
be the natural projection and
s:X\to\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
the zero section. We have a natural equivalence

\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}/X}=\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}\times_{X}\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}

provided by the group structure of
\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}
. This implies that
f^{!}_{dR^{+}}1\cong f^{*}s^{*}f^{!}_{dR^{+}}1
(resp. for
f^{alg,!}_{dR^{*}}1
). In particular, by
\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}
-invariance, we have that
f_{dR^{+},*}f^{!}_{dR^{+}}1=s^{*}f^{!}_{dR^{+}}1
(resp. for
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
).

Case of
f_{dR^{+}}^{\alg}
. We have a natural equivalence

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{dR^{+},X}^{an,alg}=(\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}(-1).

Indeed, by Lemma
5.1.10
there is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{d,\alg}=(\mathbb{G}^{d}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{d}_{a}(-1)
, this isomorphism is clearly
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant (eg. looking at the level of points) and it descends to an equivalence over the stack
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
; by base change one deduces the general case. The map
f_{dR^{+},X}^{\alg}
factors through

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{dR^{+},X}^{an,alg}\xrightarrow{h}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\widehat{\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})}(-1)\xrightarrow{g}X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m},

we find that

s^{*}f^{\alg,!}_{dR^{+},X}1=s^{*}h^{*}g^{!}1\otimes s^{*}h^{!}1.

By Theorems
3.6.15
and
4.2.5
we obtain that

s^{*}f^{\alg,!}_{dR^{+},X}1=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}(-d)[-d]=\mathscr{O}(-d).

Case of
f_{dR^{+}}
. We have a natural equivalence

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{dR^{+},X}^{\an}=\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})^{\an}/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})(-1)^{\dagger}.

Indeed, by Lemma
5.2.8
there is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{G}_{a,dR^{+}}^{d,\an}=(\mathbb{G}^{d,\an}_{a}\times\mathbb{A}^{1,\an}/\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\an})/\mathbb{G}^{d}_{a}(-1)^{\dagger}
, this isomorphism is clearly
\mathbf{GL}_{d}
-equivariant (eg. looking at the level of points) and it descends to an equivalence over the stack
*/\mathbf{GL}_{d}
; by base change one deduces the general case. The map
f_{dR^{+},X}
factors trough

\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})_{dR^{+},X}^{\an}\xrightarrow{h}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})/\mathbb{V}(\mathscr{F})(-1)^{\dagger}\xrightarrow{g}X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m},

we get

s^{*}f^{!}_{dR^{+},X}1=s^{*}h^{*}g^{!}1\otimes s^{*}h^{!}1.

By Theorems
3.6.15
and
4.3.13
and Proposition
4.2.5
, we find that

s^{*}f^{!}_{dR^{+},X}1=\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}^{\vee}[d]\otimes\bigwedge^{d}\mathscr{F}(-d)[d]=\mathscr{O}(-d)[2d].

∎

5.4.
Analytic de Rham stack of rigid spaces

Let
(K,K^{+})
be a non archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We finish with the study of the de Rham stack for rigid spaces over
(K,K^{+})
. We thank Alberto Vezzani for the questions that motivated this section. From now on all the analytic de Rham stacks are relative to
\AnSpec(K,K^{+})_{{\square}}
.

The main goal of the section is to prove the following theorem:

Theorem 5.4.1
.

Let
X
be an adic space locally of finite type over
(K,K^{+})
, then the morphism
X\to X_{dR}
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover of Tate stacks. Futhermore, if
X
is quasi-compact then
X\to X_{dR}
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover. In particular, we have that

\Mod(X_{dR})=\varprojlim_{[n]\in\Delta}\Mod(\Delta^{n+1}(X)^{\dagger}),

for both
*
and
!
-pullbacks, where
\Delta^{n+1}(X)^{\dagger}\subset X^{n+1}
is the overconvergent neighbourhood of the locally closed diagonal map, obtained as the Čech nerve of
X\to X_{dR}
.

Lemma 5.4.2
.

Let
X
be a reduced and irreducible adic space locally of finite type over
(K,K^{+})
. Then, locally in the analytic topology, there is an open Zariski subspace
U\subset X
where
\mathbb{L}_{U/K}
is a projective
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-module.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
X=\AnSpec(A,A^{+})_{{\square}}
. By Noether’s normalization lemma for rigid spaces (
[
Bos14
, §2.2 Corollary 11]
), there is a Tate algebra
B=K\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle
and an injective and finite map
B\to A
. Let
\eta\in\Spec B(*)
be the generic point of the underlying discrete ring of
B
and
\kappa(\eta)
its residue field, then
A\otimes_{B(*)}\kappa(\eta)
is finite over
B\otimes_{B(*)}\kappa(\eta)
, and the underlying discrete ring of the last is a field. Then, the underlying discrete ring of
A\otimes_{B(*)}\kappa(\eta)
is a finite field extension of
\kappa(\eta)
. By noetherian approximation we can find an element
b\in B(*)
such that
A[\frac{1}{b}]
is a finite étale extension of
B[\frac{1}{b}]
, in particular
\mathbb{L}_{A[\frac{1}{b}]/K}
is projective. We can then take
U
to be the analytification of the space
\AnSpec A[\frac{1}{b}]
.
∎

Lemma 5.4.3
.

Let
(A,A^{+})
be an Huber pair with
A
a Tate algebra of finite type over
K
. Suppose that
X^{\prime}:=\AnSpec(A,A^{0})_{{\square}}
is a solid smooth rigid space over
K
, then
X=\AnSpec(A,A^{+})_{{\square}}
is
\dagger
-smooth locally in the analytic topology of
X
. Furthermore, if
X^{\prime}
is standard smooth then
X
is
\dagger
-smooth.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.5.6
the space
X^{\prime}
is solid smooth if and only if locally in the analytic topology it is solid standard smooth. Then, we can assume without loss of generality that
(A,A^{\circ})
is solid standard smooth. Let us write
A=K\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle/(f_{1},\ldots,f_{e})
a standard smooth presentation of
A
, we want to show that
\AnSpec(A,A^{+})_{{\square}}
is
\dagger
-smooth. Since
\Spa(A,A^{+})
is an analytic open subspace of
\Spa(A,K^{+})
, it suffices to consider the case when
A^{+}
is the open integral closure of
K^{+}
in
A
. We can then write

X\xrightarrow{f}\mathbb{D}^{d-e,/K}_{K}\xrightarrow{g}\AnSpec(K,K^{+})_{{\square}},

where
\mathbb{D}^{d-e,/K}_{K}=\AnSpec(K\langle T_{e+1},\ldots,T_{d}\rangle,K^{+})_{{\square}}
. Then, it suffices to show that
f
is
\dagger
-étale and that
g
is
\dagger
-smooth. The fact that
g
is
\dagger
-smooth follows from the fact that the polynomial algebra is a compact projective analytic ring, and by invariance of the bounded condition for analytic rings of Proposition
2.6.16
. The proof that
f
is
\dagger
-formally étale follows exactly the same argument of Proposition
3.7.5
, we left the details to the reader.
∎

Lemma 5.4.4
.

Let
X
be a reduced affinoid adic space of finite type over
(K,K^{+})
, and suppose that there is a locally Zariski open subspace
U\subset X
with reduced complement
Z
such that
U\to U_{dR}
and
Z\to Z_{dR}
are descendable, then
X\to X_{dR}
is descendable.

Proof.

Let us write
X=\AnSpec(A,A^{+})
and let
I\subset A
be the ideal of definition of
Z
. Let
X^{\dagger/Z}=\AnSpec(A^{\dagger/Z},A^{+})
be the
\dagger
-formal completion of
X
at
Z
. We have a morphism of Tate stacks

Z\to X^{\dagger/Z}\to Z_{dR}.

Since
Z\to Z_{dR}
is desendable, then
X^{\dagger/Z}\to Z_{dR}
is also descendable. On the other hand, we have an excision sequence of de Rham stacks

j_{dR}:U_{dR}\subset X_{dR}\supset Z_{dR}:\iota_{dR},

we then have a fiber sequence

(5.5)

j_{dR,!}1_{U_{dR}}\to 1_{X_{dR}}\to\iota_{dR,*}1_{Z_{dR}}.

We also have an excision

j:U\subset X\supset X^{\dagger/Z}:\iota

giving rise to a fiber sequence

j_{!}1_{U}\to 1_{X}\to\iota_{*}\iota_{*}1_{X^{\dagger/Z}}.

Note that we have cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\dagger/Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{dR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{dR}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{dR}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{X^{\dagger/Z}}}

This shows that
\iota_{dR}^{*}f_{X}1_{X}=f_{X^{\dagger/Z},*}1_{X^{\dagger}/Z}
. Then, by the projection formula of
\iota_{dR,*}
and since
f_{X^{\dagger/Z}}
is descendable, one deduces that
\iota_{dR,*}1_{Z_{dR}}
belongs to the thick tensor ideal generated by
f_{dR,*}1_{X}
. Similarly,
j_{dR,!}(f_{U,*}1_{U})
is in the thick tensor ideal of
f_{X,*}1_{X}
(being the fiber of
f_{X,*}1_{X}\to\iota_{dR,*}f_{X^{\dagger/Z},*}1_{X^{\dagger}/Z}
), and by the projection formula and descendability of
f_{U}
, then so is
j_{dR,!}1_{U,dR}
. Therefore, by (
5.5
), we get that
1_{X_{dR}}
is in the thick tensor ideal of
f_{X,*}1_{X}
, proving that
X\to X_{dR}
is descendable by
[
Mat16
, Definition 3.18]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.4.1
.

By taking affinoid covers, we can assume without loss of generality that
X
is quasi-compact and separated. Writing
X
as union of reduced irreducible spaces, we can assume by Lemma
5.4.4
that
X
is irreducible. Moreover, let
\mathcal{I}
be the nilpotent radical of
X
, since
X
is quasi-compact
\mathcal{I}
is nilpotent, and the map
X^{\red}\to X
is descendable (see
[
Mat16
, Proposition 3.35]
). Therefore, we can assume that
X
is reduced and irreducible. We proceed to prove the theorem by induction on the dimension of
X
, the zero dimensional case being trivial.

By Lemma
5.4.2
there is a locally open Zariski subspace
U\subset X
such that
\mathbb{L}_{X/K}
is a projective
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-module, we let
Z
be its Zariski closed complement. By induction in the dimension,
Z\to Z_{dR}
is descendable, then by Lemma
5.4.4
it suffices to show that
U\to U_{dR}
is descendable.

Let
U^{\prime}\subset U
be the maximal rigid space contained in
U
, by Theorem
3.5.6

U^{\prime}
is solid smooth over
K
, and Lemma
5.4.3
implies that
U
is
\dagger
-smooth locally in the analytic topology. Then, Proposition
5.2.3
and Theorem
5.1.13
show that
U\to U_{dR}
is descendable thanks to the Hodge filtration of the de Rham complex. This finish the proof of the theorem.
∎

Corollary 5.4.5
.

Let
X
be an adic space locally of finite type over
(K,K^{+})
and let
j:X^{\prime}\subset X
be maximal rigid space contained in
X
. Let
j_{dR}:X^{\prime}_{dR}\to X_{dR}
be the associated maps at the level of de Rham stacks. Then the natural map

1_{X_{dR}}\xrightarrow{\sim}j_{dR,*}1_{X^{\prime}_{dR}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
X=\AnSpec(A,A^{+})
is affinoid, so that
X^{\prime}=\AnSpec(A,A^{0})
. By Theorem
5.4.1
we have
\mathscr{D}
-covers
f:X\to X_{dR}
and
g:X^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}_{dR}
. Consider the Čech nerves
(\Delta^{n+1}(X)^{\dagger})_{[n]\in\Delta}
and
(\Delta^{n+1}(X^{\prime})^{\dagger})_{[n]\in\Delta}
of
f
and
g
respectively, where
\Delta^{n}(Z)^{\dagger}
is the overconvergent neighbourhood of the diagonal
\Delta^{n}(Z)\subset Z^{n}
. We have a map of simplicial affinoid spaces

j^{\bullet}:\Delta^{\bullet+1}(X^{\prime})^{\dagger}\to\Delta^{\bullet+1}(X)^{\dagger}.

Note that the underlying condensed rings of
\Delta^{n+1}(X^{\prime})^{\dagger}
and
\Delta^{n+1}(X)^{\dagger}
are the same: this follows from the fact that the underlying condensed rings of
X^{{}^{\prime}n+1}
and
X^{n+1}
are the same for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and that the overconvergent diagonal is defined using the same ideal of definition. This implies that
j^{\bullet}_{*}1_{\Delta^{\bullet+1}(X^{\prime})}=1_{\Delta^{\bullet+1}(X)}
is a cocartesian section. Then, since

\Mod(X_{dR})=\varprojlim_{[n]\in\Delta}\Mod(\Delta^{n+1}(X)^{\dagger})

and

\Mod(X^{\prime}_{dR})=\varprojlim_{[n]\in\Delta}\Mod(\Delta^{n+1}(X^{\prime})^{\dagger}),

one deduces that
1_{X_{dR}}\xrightarrow{\sim}j_{dR,*}1_{X^{\prime}_{dR}}
is an equivalence as wanted.
∎

6.
Analytic de Rham stack and locally analytic representations

The last section of this paper concerns the relation between the analytic de Rham stack, the theory of locally analytic representations as in
[
RJRC22
,
RJRC23
]
, and the theory of equivariant
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules of
[
Ard21
]
. In §
6.1
we introduce smooth
\dagger
-groupoids for derived Tate adic spaces. Geometric realizations of these kind of groupoids generalize the construction of the de Rham stack for solid smooth morphisms. Then, in §
6.2
, we use the notion of smooth
\dagger
-groupoid together with actions of
p
-adic Lie groups to give a very general notion of equivariant analytic
D
-module.

6.1.
Smooth
\dagger
-groupoids

Different theories of
D
-modules over rigid spaces
X
are built up from different epimorphisms of
\mathscr{D}
-stacks
X\to X^{\prime}
, equivalently, from different groupoid objects living over
X
. In the case of analytic
D
-modules, the kind of groupoid objects we encounter have a special shape, namely, they look like non-commutative deformations of the trivial group object
X\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger,d}\to X
for some
d\geq 1
, where
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger}=\AnSpec\mathbb{Q}_{p}\{T\}^{\dagger}
. The previous observation leads us to the notion of a smooth
\dagger
-groupoid over a derived Tate adic space.

We start by briefly recalling the definition of a groupoid object in an
\infty
-category as well as some related notions. Then, we introduce smooth
\dagger
-groupoids on derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and prove some cohomological properties of them. We end with some examples appearing in the theory of twisted
D
-modules of rigid spaces.

6.1.1.
Groupoids

Definition 6.1.1

(
[
Lur09
, Definition 6.1.2.7]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits. A groupoid object on
\mathscr{C}
is a simplicial object
\mathcal{G}:\Delta^{\op}\to\mathscr{C}
such that for all
[n]\in\Delta
and all partition
[n]=S\bigcup S^{\prime}
with
S\cap S^{\prime}=\{s\}
, the natural map

\mathcal{G}([n])\to\mathcal{G}(S)\times_{\mathcal{G}(s)}\mathcal{G}(S^{\prime})

is an equivalence. We let
\mathcal{G}_{\bullet}
denote the groupoid object in
\mathscr{C}
. We call
\mathcal{G}_{0}
the objects of the groupoid, the map
d_{1}:\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{0}
the source map and
d_{0}:\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{0}
the target map. By an abuse of notation we say that
\mathcal{G}
is a groupoid over
X:=\mathcal{G}_{0}
, if
\mathscr{C}
admits geometric realizations we denote

X/\mathcal{G}:=\varinjlim_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}\mathcal{G}_{\bullet}.

Let
G
be a group, a standard procedure to construct more groups from
G
is to take quotients
G/H
by normal subgroups. It turns out that being "normal" for a map of groups in higher category theory is not longer a property but additional datum:

Definition 6.1.2

(Normal map of groupoids)
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-topos with effective epimorphisms and let
\mathcal{G}
be groupoid over
X
in
\mathcal{C}
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be a group object over
X
and
f:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{G}
a morphism of groupoids over
X
with geometric realizations
X/\mathcal{H}\to X/\mathcal{G}
. A
normal quotient
of
f
is the datum of a pullback square in
\mathscr{C}_{X/}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\mathcal{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

such that
X\to Y
is an epimorphism. By an abuse of notation we let
\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{H}
denote the groupoid associated to the epimorphism
X\to Y
.

6.1.2.
\dagger
-groupoids

We let
\mathscr{C}=\AdicSp_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
denote the category of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Definition 6.1.3
.

Let
X\in\mathscr{C}
, let
\mathcal{G}
be a groupoid in
\mathcal{C}
over
X
, and let
X/\mathcal{G}
be its geometric realization.

(1)

We say that
\mathcal{G}
is a
\dagger
-groupoid
if the topological simplicial object
|\mathcal{G}_{\bullet}|
is the constant object
|X|
, i.e. if for all map
[n]\to[m]
we have an homeomorphism of topological spaces
|\mathcal{G}_{m}|\xrightarrow{\sim}|\mathcal{G}_{n}|
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
\dagger
-groupoid over
X
. We say that
\mathcal{G}
is
smooth of relative dimension
d
if the target map
d_{0}:\mathcal{G}_{1}\to X
is, locally in the analytic topology of
X
, equivalent to the projection
X\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger,d}\to X
.

Remark 6.1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
\dagger
-groupoid over
X
. The fact that any object
\mathcal{G}_{n}
has the same underlying topological space implies that we can localize the groupoid in the analytic topology of
X
. Namely, for any open subspace
U\subset X
, the preimages
U_{n}
of the map
d_{0}:\mathcal{G}_{m}\to X
define a subsimplicial object
U_{\bullet}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\bullet}
that is clearly a groupoid over
U
.

The following proposition implies that geometric realizations of smooth
\dagger
-groupoids have a well behaved theory of six functors.

Proposition 6.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a smooth
\dagger
-groupoid over
X
, then the natural map
f:X\to X/\mathcal{G}
is a descendable
\mathscr{D}
-cover.

Proof.

We have a cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\mathcal{G}_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}

Therefore, locally in the analytic topology of
X
, the arrow
X\to X/\mathcal{G}
has fibers given by
\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\dagger,d}
. Thus, we have a natural equivalence

f_{dR}:X_{dR,X/\mathcal{G}}\xrightarrow{\sim}X/\mathcal{G}

and the de Rham cohomology
1_{X/\mathcal{G}}=f_{dR,*}1_{X_{dR,X/\mathcal{G}}}
is Hodge complete. By Proposition
5.2.3
,
1_{X/\mathcal{G}}
has by Hodge graduation (after forgetting the weight)

gr^{i}(1_{X/\mathcal{G}})=f_{*}\Omega^{i}_{X/(X/\mathcal{G})}.

Since
\Omega^{i}_{X/(X/\mathcal{G})}
is locally free in the analytic topology of
X
, one has descendability of
f
as wanted.
∎

Let us now focus in the case of a smooth
\dagger
-group
G
over a derived Tate adic space
X
, namely, group objects over
X
that are in addition smooth
\dagger
-groupoids.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

Let
X
be a derived Tate adic space,
G
a group object over
X
in derived Tate adic spaces, and
e:X\to G
the unit section. Then the co-lie complex
e^{*}\mathbb{L}_{G/X}
has a natural structure of
G
-module defining an object
\ell_{G/X}\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X/G)
. Moreover, if
f:X\to X/G
, then there is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{L}_{X/(X/G)}\cong f^{*}\ell_{X/G}
.

Proof.

The diagonal map
G\to G\times G
induces a morphism of classifying stacks

(6.1)

\Delta:X/G\to X/(G\times G).

We have a natural equivalence
X/G\xrightarrow{\sim}G/(G\times G)
, where
X\to G
is the unit map,
G\to G\times G
is the diagonal map, and
G\times G
acts on
G
by
(g_{1},g_{2})\cdot g=g_{1}gg_{2}^{-1}
. Then, the arrow (
6.1
) is equivalent to the map

G/(G\times G)\to X/(G\times G).

This implies that the underlying object of
\mathbb{L}_{\Delta}
is precisely
\ell_{G/X}
, and that
G
acts on
\ell_{G/X}
by the adjoint action. The last statement follows from the previous computation and the following cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/(G\times G),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}

where
h
corresponds to the map of groups
(\id,e):G\to G\times G
.
∎

Proposition 6.1.7
.

Let
G
be a smooth
\dagger
-group over
X
of relative dimension
d
. Then the map
g:X/G\to X
is cohomologically smooth and there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}1_{X}\cong\bigwedge^{d}\ell_{G/X}^{\vee}[d]
.

Proof.

Let
f:X\to X/G
, by Proposition
6.1.5
the map
g
admits
!
-functors and is weakly cohomologically proper. In order to show that
g
is cohomologically smooth we first compute the right adjoint
g^{!}
. The proof of Proposition
6.1.5
produced a Hodge filtration for the unit object
1_{X/G}
. By proper base change, we have Hodge filtrations for all
M\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X/G)
such that

\gr^{i}(M)=f_{*}f^{*}(\bigwedge^{i}\ell_{G/X}\otimes M).

Let
N\in\Mod_{{\square}}(X)
. The
\mathrm{Hom}
space

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X}(g_{*}M,N)

has a filtration with graded pieces

\displaystyle gr^{-i}(R\mathrm{Hom}_{X}(g_{*}M,N))

\displaystyle=R\mathrm{Hom}_{X}(g_{*}(f_{*}f^{*}(\bigwedge^{i}\ell_{G/X}\otimes M)),N)

\displaystyle=R\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X}(f^{*}(\bigwedge^{i}\ell_{G/X}\otimes M),N)

\displaystyle=R\mathrm{Hom}_{X/G}(M,\bigwedge^{i}\ell^{\vee}_{G/X}\otimes f_{*}N)

\displaystyle=R\mathrm{Hom}_{X/G}(M,f_{*}f^{*}(\bigwedge^{i}\ell^{\vee}_{G/X}\otimes g^{*}N)).

But this filtration is also induced by the dual of the Hodge-filtration of
g^{*}N
which is nothing but a
\bigwedge^{d}\ell_{G/X}^{\vee}[d]
-twist of the Hodge filtration. This shows that there is a natural equivalence
g^{!}N\cong\bigwedge^{d}\ell_{G/X}^{\vee}[d]\otimes g^{*}N
. We still need to prove that
g
is cohomologically smooth, for this we employ Lemma
4.2.2
. We let
\mathcal{L}=g^{!}1_{X}=\bigwedge^{d}\ell^{\ell}_{G/X}[d]
. The Hodge filtration gives rise a map
f_{*}1_{X}\to\mathcal{L}
, and the adjunction
g_{*}\mathcal{L}=g_{*}g^{!}1_{X}\to 1_{X}
produces a splitting
1_{X}\to g_{*}\mathcal{L}\to 1_{X}
. Thus, we have all the data and hypothesis needed in Lemma
4.2.2
, proving that
g
is cohomologically smooth.
∎

Example 6.1.8
.

In the following we give some examples of smooth
\dagger
-groups and groupoids that appear in the theory of analytic
D
-modules.

(1)

Let
f:X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived rigid spaces. Since
X\to Y
is formally
\dagger
-smooth in the analytic topology of
Y
, (cf. Proposition
3.7.5
), the map
X\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
is an epimorphism by Proposition
3.7.5
. Then, by Proposition
3.7.5
one deduces that the Čech nerve of
X\to X_{dR^{+},Y}
is equal to
(\Delta^{n+1,\dagger}_{Y}X)_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
, where
\Delta^{n}_{Y}:X\to X^{\times_{Y}n}
is the diagonal map, and where
\Delta^{n,\dagger}_{Y}X\subset X^{\times_{Y}n}
is the immersion attached to the locally closed Zariski immersion
|\Delta^{n}_{Y}(X)|\subset|X^{\times_{Y}n}|
. Then, locally in the analytic topology of
Y
and
X
, the morphism
f
is standard solid smooth and by taking a factorization
X\to Y\times\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}\to Y
with the first arrow being standard solid étale, one gets that
(\Delta^{n+1,\dagger}_{Y}X)_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
is a smooth
\dagger
-groupoid over
X
by Lemma
5.2.8
(1). Moreover, the previous description holds for any locally closed subspace of
X
in the sense of locale for the analytic topology.

(2)

Let
X
be a derived Tate adic space and let
G
be a group object over
X
such that
G\to X
is, locally in the analytic topology of
X
and
G
, a locally closed subspace of a solid smooth map. Let
\exp(\Lie G)^{\dagger}\subset G

be the locally closed subspace associated to the unit map
|X|\to|G|
. Then there is a natural equivalence
G_{dR,X}=G/\exp(\Lie G)^{\dagger}
. Indeed, by (1)
G_{dR,X}
is the geometric realization of the overconvergent diagonals of the Čech nerve of
G\to X
, but the Čech nerve of
G\to X
is equivalent to the simplicial space
(G^{\times_{X}n+1})_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
that encodes the group structure of
G
, and the Čech nerve of the de Rham stack of
G
corresponds to the subspace given by
(\exp(\Lie G)^{\dagger,\times_{X}n}\times G)_{[n]\in\Delta^{\op}}
, whose geometric realization is precisely
G/\exp(\Lie G)^{\dagger}
.

(3)

Let
(K,K^{+})
be a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
X
be a rigid space over
(K,K^{+})
, seen as a derived Tate adic space over
\AnSpec(K,K^{+})_{{\square}}
. Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a
K
-linear Lie algebroid over
X
(cf.
[
AW19
, §9.1]
) which is locally finite free in the analytic topology of
X
. Let
U(\mathfrak{L})
be its enveloping algebra over
\mathscr{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
its algebra of locally analytic distributions, i.e. the Fréchet completion of
[
AW19
, §9.3]
. The diagonal map
\mathfrak{L}\to\mathfrak{L}\oplus\mathfrak{L}
defines a commutative co-algebra structure on
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
compatible with its algebra structure, that endows
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
with a Hopf-algebra structure over
K
. Let us fix the left
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-action on
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
. Taking duals with respect to
\mathscr{O}_{X}
of the natural projection
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})/\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})(\mathfrak{L})\cong\mathscr{O}_{X}
we get a morphism of commutative algebras
d^{0}:\mathscr{O}_{X}\to C^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{L})
. By fixing a basis of
\mathfrak{L}
over
\mathscr{O}_{X}
, the Poincaré-Birkhoff-Witt theorem implies that
C^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{L})
is isomorphic to
\mathscr{O}_{X}\{T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\}^{\dagger}
, where
d
is the rank of
\mathfrak{L}
over
\mathscr{O}_{X}
. On the other hand, the orbit map
d^{1}:\mathscr{O}_{X}\to C^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{L})
obtained by the action of
\mathfrak{L}
on
\mathscr{O}_{X}
is also a morphism of commutative algebras. The natural map
\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
induces an augmentation map
s:C^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{L})\to\mathscr{O}_{X}
. Taking analytic spectrum over
X
we end up with the data of a
(\leq 1)
-simplicial space

(6.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exp(\mathfrak{L})^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

with
\exp(\mathfrak{L})^{\dagger}=\AnSpec_{X}C^{\dagger}(\mathfrak{L})
. It is not hard to see that the Lie algebra structure of
\mathfrak{L}
defines a groupoid object structure on (
6.2
), we call this groupoid the
exponential of
\mathfrak{L}
. We also call
\Mod_{{\square}}(X/\exp(\mathfrak{L})^{\dagger})
the category of
analytic
U(\mathfrak{L})
or
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
-modules
.

(4)

In the notation of the previous point, let
X
be a smooth rigid space over
(K,K^{+})
. Then the tangent space
\mathcal{T}_{X/K}
has a natural structure of Lie algebroid over
X
. The exponential
\exp(\mathcal{T}_{X/K})^{\dagger}
is nothing but the Čech nerve of the de Rham stack of
X
. Indeed, it suffices to prove this locally in the analytic topology of
X
, and we can assume that we have an étale map towards a relative polydisc over
K
. By naturality under étale maps, it suffices to prove it for
\mathbb{G}_{a,{\square}}^{d}
, which follows from the case of group objects of point (2).

(5)

Let
(K,K^{+})
be a non-archimedean extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and let
\bf{G}
be a reductive group over
K
. Let
\bf{P}\subset\bf{G}
be a parabolic subgroup, let
\bf{N}\to\bf{P}\to\bf{M}
be the short exact sequence of its unipotent radical and the Levi quotient, and let
\Fl=\bf{P}\backslash\bf{G}
be the flag variety. For a group
\bf{H}
we let
\mathfrak{h}
denote its Lie algebra. There is a natural action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
\Fl
by derivations, this defines a Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}^{0}:=\mathscr{O}_{\Fl}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
over
\Fl
whose exponential is the smooth
\dagger
groupoid
\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}\times\Fl\to\Fl
induced by the natural multiplication. The Lie algebras
\mathfrak{n}
and
\mathfrak{p}
have a natural adjoint action by
\bf{P}
, and they define Lie algebroids
\mathfrak{n}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{0}
. In fact, these Lie algebroids act trivially on
\mathscr{O}_{\Fl}
and they are ideals of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
, thus the associated smooth
\dagger
-groupoids
\exp(\mathfrak{n}^{0})^{\dagger}
and
\exp(\mathfrak{p}^{0})^{\dagger}
are normal subgroups of
\exp(\mathfrak{g}^{0})^{\dagger}
(they are actually normal subgroups of the bigger groupoid
\mathbf{G}^{\an}\times\Fl\to\Fl
, where
\mathbf{G}^{\an}
is the analytitfication of
\bf{G}
to a rigid space). The quotient
\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{p}^{0}
is the tangent space of
\Fl
, this implies that we have a fiber sequence

\Fl/\exp(\mathfrak{p}^{0})^{\dagger}\to\Fl/\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}\to\Fl_{dR}.

On the other hand, we call
\Fl/(\exp(\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}))^{\dagger}
the universal twisted analytic de Rham stack of
\Fl
, and call
\Mod_{{\square}}(\Fl/(\exp(\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}))^{\dagger})
the category of analytic universal twisted
D
-modules of
\Fl
.

6.2.
p
-adic Lie groups and analytic
D
-modules

We end this section with the relation between analytic
D
-modules, locally analytic representations of
p
-adic Lie groups, and the theory of equivariant twisted
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}
-modules. We need some notations.

Definition 6.2.1
.

Let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group. We let
G
,
G^{sm}
and
G^{la}
denote the analytic adic spaces obtained by sending a compact open subspace
U\subset G
to the spaces
C(U,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
C^{sm}(U,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
C^{la}(U,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of continuous, locally constant, and locally analytic functions of
U
.

The following lemma provides a clean relation between the groups
G
,
G^{la}
and
G^{sm}
.

Lemma 6.2.2
.

Let
G^{\dagger}\subset G
be the closed immersion of locales corresponding to the unit section. Then
G^{\dagger}
is a normal subgroup of
G
and there is a natural equivalence
G/G^{\dagger}=G^{sm}
. Similarly, we have that
G^{la}_{dR}=G^{la}/\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}=G^{sm}
.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
G
is compact. We can write
G^{\dagger}=\varprojlim_{1\in H\subset G}H
where
H
runs over all compact open subgroups of
G
. Then one finds that

G/G^{\dagger}=\varprojlim_{H}G/H=G^{sm}.

For the claim about
G^{la}
, by Example
6.1.8
(2) we have that
G^{la}_{dR}=G^{la}/\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}
. We can also write
\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}=\varprojlim_{H}H^{la}
where
H
runs over all the open compact subgroups of
G
. One finds that

G^{la}_{dR}=\varprojlim_{H}G^{la}/H^{la}=\varprojlim_{H}G/H=G^{sm}.

∎

Next we show that the classifying stacks of
G
,
G^{la}
and
G^{sm}
have
!
-functors.

Proposition 6.2.3
.

Let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group. The maps
*\to*/G
,
*\to*/G^{la}
and
*\to*/G^{sm}
are
\mathscr{D}
-covers. Furthermore, if
G
is compact they are descendable
\mathscr{D}
-covers.

Proof.

Let
H\subset G
be an open and compact subgroup, the natural map
*/H\to*/G
is fibered on
G/H
which is discrete over
*
, so cohomologically étale. Thus, to show that
*\to*/G
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover it suffices to show that
*\to*/H
is a
\mathscr{D}
-cover (resp. for
H^{la}
and
H^{sm}
), so we can assume that
G
is compact. Let us write
f
for any of the projections of
*
to the classifying stacks. In the case of
G^{sm}
, the object
f_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is nothing but the algebra
C^{sm}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of smooth functions endowed with the left regular action. Since
C^{sm}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
admits
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as an equivariant direct summand, we get that
*\to*/G^{sm}
is descendable. Descendability for
G
and
G^{la}
follows from the Lazard-Serre resolution (
[
RJRC22
, Theorems 5.7 and 5.8]
), namely, the Lazard-Serre resolution is a long exact sequence of
\mathbb{Z}_{p,{\square}}[G]
-modules

0\to\mathbb{Z}_{p,{\square}}[G]^{\dim G}\to\cdots\to\mathbb{Z}_{p,{\square}}[G]\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to 0

which by a theorem of Kolhaase extends to a long exact sequence of the locally analytic distribution algebra of
G
:

0\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(G)^{\dim G}\to\cdots\to\mathcal{D}^{la}(G)\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to 0.

Taking duals with respect to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we got long exact sequences of representations of
G

0\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to C(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\cdots\to C(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\dim G}\to 0

and

0\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to C^{la}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\cdots\to C^{la}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\dim G}\to 0,

which proves descendability of
*\to*/G
and
*\to*/G^{la}
respectively.
∎

We now study cohomological properties of the classifying stacks of
G
and
G^{la}
.

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group and consider the maps
f:*/G^{sm}\to*
,
g:*/G\to*
and
h:*/G^{la}\to*
. Then
f
,
g
and
h
are cohomologically smooth, both
g^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
h^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
are naturally isomorphic to
\bigwedge^{\dim G}\mathfrak{g}[d]
, and
f^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=\delta_{G}
is the unimodular character.

Proof.

We can assume without loss of generality that
G
is compact. Indeed, given
H
a compact open subgroup of
G
, the Čech nerve
X_{\bullet}
of the map
*/H\to*/G
is given by
X_{n}=H\backslash G\times^{H}\cdots\times^{H}G/H
(
n
-copies of
G
), and all the arrows
X_{n}\to X_{m}
are cohomologically étale (resp. for
G^{la}
). Thus, all the maps
g_{n}:X_{n}\to*
would be cohomologically smooth and one has natural isomorphisms
g_{n}^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=d_{0}^{*}g_{H}^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
where
g_{H}:*/H\to*
, proving that the object
g^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is already determined by its restriction to
*/H
. An explicit but tedious bookkeeping of the maps in the Čech nerve will show that the action is the adjoint for
G
and
G^{la}
, and the unimordular action for
G^{sm}
(see
[
HKW22
, Example 4.2.4]
).

Now let us suppose that
G
is compact, we can even assume that
G
is a uniform pro-
p
-group and fix a coordinate system
G\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{d}
. We first deal with
f
. By
[
RJRC23
, Theorem 5.4.2]
the category
\Mod(*/G^{sm})
is equivalent to the category of solid smooth representations. In particular,
f_{*}
is identified with the invariant functor which is exact, and has by right adjoint the formation of the trivial representations, namely,
f^{*}
. Then, Lemma
4.2.2
can be applied with
\mathcal{L}=f^{*}\mathbb{Q}
being the trivial representation, proving that
f
is cohomologically smooth.

Finally, we deal with
g
and
h
. By the Lazard-Serre resolution, we know that both
g
and
h
are cohomologically smooth, namely,
g_{*}
and
h_{*}
are group cohomology, and their right adjoints are the trivial representation after twisting by a character, see
[
RJRC22
, Theorem 5.19]
(one can also apply Lemma
4.2.2
with
\mathcal{L}
to the line bundle
\chi=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p,{\square}}[G])
). It is left to compute the dualizing sheaf
\chi
of the classifying stacks. Let
\mathbb{G}^{(h)}
be the affinoid group consisting on finitely many disjoint affinoid polydiscs of radius
p^{-h}
around the elements of
g\in G
, we also let
\overline{\mathbb{G}}^{(h)}=\varprojlim_{h^{\prime}<h}\mathbb{G}^{(h^{\prime})}
be the overconvergent affinoid group of radius
p^{-h}
. Then, since the Lazard-Serre resolution is already extended for analytic distribution algebras (see proof of
[
Koh11
, Theorem 4.4]
), letting
f:*/\overline{\mathbb{G}}^{(h)}\to*
and
k:*/G^{la}\to*/\overline{\mathbb{G}}^{(h)}
, we have that
h^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=k^{*}f^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
for some
h>>0
. Hence, it suffices to compute the dualizing sheaf of the map
f
. By taking the connected component of the identity, we are reduced to compute the dualizing sheaf of the classifying stack of an affinoid group
\overline{\mathbb{G}}
whose underlying adic space is a closed polydisc of dimension
d
, this follows the same argument as Proposition
6.1.7
obtaining
\bigwedge^{d}\Lie\overline{\mathbb{G}}[d]
endowed with its adjoint action. This finishes the proof.
∎

With the previous preparations we can finally define equivariant
D
-modules on derived Tate adic spaces.

Definition 6.2.5
.

Let
X\to Y
be a morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group and suppose that we have an action of
G^{la}
on
X
over
Y
. Let
\exp(\mathfrak{g}^{0})^{\dagger}
denote the groupoid over
X
obtained by the restriction of the action of
G^{la}
to
\exp(\mathfrak{g})^{\dagger}
. Let
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}\to\exp(\mathfrak{g}^{0})^{\dagger}
be a normal morphism of groupoids such that the composite
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}\to G^{la}
is also normal. We define the category of analytic equivariant
D(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})
-modules to be
\Mod_{{\square}}(X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}))
.

Finally, the following theorem computes dualizing sheaves for equivariant analytic
D
-modules of solid smooth morphisms.

Theorem 6.2.6
.

Let
X\to Y
be a solid smooth morphism of derived Tate adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
of relative dimension
d
, and let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group of dimension
g
acting locally analytically on
X
over
Y
. Let us denote
\mathfrak{g}=\Lie G
. Let
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
be a
\dagger
-smooth group over
X
of relative dimension
e
, let
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}\to G^{la}\times X
be a map of groupoids with given normal quotient
G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
. Then
g:X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})\to Y
is cohomologically smooth and its underlying
G^{la}
-equivariant dualizing sheaf is equivalent to

g^{!}1_{Y}=\Omega^{d}_{X/Y}[d]\otimes\bigwedge^{g}\mathfrak{g}[g]\otimes\bigwedge^{e}\ell_{\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}/X}[-e].

Proof.

By hypothesis, the map
h:X/G^{la}\to X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})
is an epimorphism fibered on
X/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
. Then, the pullback along
h
is conservative and it is cohomologically smooth by Proposition
6.1.7
. Therefore,
h
is a smooth
\mathscr{D}
-cover and by Corollary
3.1.26

g
is cohomologically smooth if
g\circ h
is so. On the other hand, we can write

X/G^{la}\xrightarrow{f}Y/G^{la}\xrightarrow{k}Y,

the map
f
is representable by a solid smooth map so it is cohomologically smooth, and the map
k
is cohomologically smooth by Proposition
6.2.4
. Finally, it is left to compute the pullback of
g^{!}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
along
h
. Since
g\circ h=k\circ f
we find that

f^{*}k^{!}1_{Y}\otimes f^{!}1_{Y/G^{la}}=h^{*}g^{!}1_{Y}\otimes h^{!}1_{X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})}.

Therefore,

h^{*}g^{!}1_{Y}=f^{*}k^{!}1_{Y}\otimes f^{!}1_{Y/G^{la}}\otimes(h^{!}1_{X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})})^{-1}.

The theorem follows since
k^{!}1_{Y}=\bigwedge^{g}\mathfrak{g}[g]
by Proposition
6.2.4
,
f^{!}1_{Y/G^{la}}=\Omega^{d}_{X/Y}[d]
by Theorem
3.6.15
, and
h^{!}1_{X/(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})}=\bigwedge^{e}\ell_{\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}/X}^{\vee}[e]
by Proposition
6.1.7
since
h
is fibered on
X/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
.
∎

Example 6.2.7
.

Let
X\to Y
be a smooth morphism of rigid spaces and let
G
be a
p
-adic Lie group acting on
X
over
Y
. The action of
G
on
X
is locally analytic and extends to an action of
G^{la}
. Let
\alpha:\mathscr{O}_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\to\mathcal{T}_{X/Y}
be the anchor map, and let us assume that it is surjective. Let
\mathfrak{k}^{0}=\ker(\alpha)
, then
\mathfrak{k}^{0}
is a locally finite free Lie algebroid over
X
acting trivially on
\mathscr{O}_{X}
, and it defines a group object
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
over
X
. Furthermore, since
\mathfrak{k}^{0}
is a
G
-equivariant sheaf, the map
\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}\to G^{la}\times X
is a normal map of
1
-groupoids and we can perform the groupoid quotient
G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger}
. Then, the category of analytic
D(G^{la}/\mathbb{H}^{\dagger})
-modules will be an enhancement of the category of equivariant
\wideparen{\mathcal{D}}(X,G)
-modules of
[
Ard21
]
. A concrete relation between these two categories is left to a future work.
